The banks’ liabilities as at 31.12.2021 amounted to MDL 99,104.8
million, increasing by MDL 13,208.2 million (15.4%) compared to
31.12.2020. The largest share in total liabilities as at 31.12.2021
is held by ﬁnancial liabilities valued at amortized cost (customer
deposits, other ﬁnancial liabilities) – 97.8% (MDL 96,944.2 million),
increasing in absolute value by MDL 12,786.6 million (15.2%)
compared to the end of the previous year.
Customer deposits accounted for MDL 90,145.8 million or 91.0%
of total liabilities, with the absolute value of customer deposits
increasing
by
MDL
10,446.7
million
(13.1%)
compared
to
31.12.2020. Other ﬁnancial debts also increased by MDL 2,339.9
million (52.5%) to MDL 6,798.4 million (including loans (overnight,
short and medium term) from the NBM, the Government,
international ﬁnancial organizations, etc.), other liabilities – by
MDL 263.2 million (23.6%) to MDL 1,377.4 million (including
transit
and
clearing
amounts,
payment
card
operations,
settlements with bank employees, other individuals and legal
entities,
settlements
with
the

settlements
on

for business restructuring, liabilities related to pensions and
employee beneﬁts, provisions related to expenses for damages

The share of large banks’ liabilities in total liabilities of the
banking sector as of 31.12.2021 constituted 80.7%, increasing by
2.0 percentage points compared to 31.12.2020. Respectively, the
share
of
medium-sized
banks’
liabilities
decreased
by
1.9
percentage points, constituting 16.5% as of 31.12.2021, and the
share of small banks’ liabilities decreased insigniﬁcantly by 0.1
percentage points, constituting 2.8% in total liabilities in the
sector.
The concentration of banking sector liabilities is shown in chart
4.16.
The balance of deposits, according to prudential reports as at
31.12.2021,
amounted to MDL 90,081.9 million,
increasing
compared to 31.12.2020 by MDL 10,443.2 million (13.1%), due to
the increase in deposits of legal entities by MDL 5,294.0 million
(18.4%) to MDL 34,015.4 million, deposits of individuals by MDL
5,148.9 million (10.1%) to MDL 55,918.4 million and deposits of
banks by MDL 0.2 million (0.2%) to MDL 148.1 million.
The total share of deposits in GDP decreased from 39.9% in 2020
to 37.2% in 2021.
As of 31.12.2021, in total deposits, 42.6% (equivalent to MDL
38,386.4 million) are deposits in foreign currency, which absolute
value increased compared to the end of the previous year by
MDL 5,070.9 million (15.2%), including from attracting funds –
by MDL 5,900.3 million, while due to revaluation as a result of
exchange rate ﬂuctuations they decreased by MDL 901.9 million
(the calculation from the revaluation of deposits was made based
on EUR, USD, Russian ruble, Romanian leu and Ukrainian hryvnia,
the share of other currencies being insigniﬁcant).
Deposits in
Moldovan lei accounted for 57.4% of total deposits or MDL 51,695.5
million, increasing by MDL 5,372.3 million (11.6%) compared to
the previous year.
The value of the long-term liquidity indicator in the banking sector
(principle I of liquidity, assets with a repayment term of more than
two years/ﬁnancial resources with a potential withdrawal term of
more than two years) was 0.74 (sector average), ranging from
0.35 to 0.89, practically at the same level as at the end of the
previous year. The given indicator was respected by all banks
(limit per bank ≤1).
The value of the current liquidity indicator in the sector – principle
II of liquidity (liquid assets, expressed in cash, deposits with the
NBM, liquid securities, net interbank funds with maturity up to
one month/total assets x 100%) was 48.6% (sector average),
ranging from 34.6% to 63.3%, decreasing by 2.1 percentage points
compared to the end of the previous year. The given indicator was
met by all banks (limit per bank ≥20%).
As of 31.12.2021, the principle III of liquidity, which shows the ratio
of adjusted effective liquidity to required liquidity in each maturity


74
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
band (limit ≥1), was met by all banks, ranging from 1.69 in the
maturity band up to and including one month to 120.89 in the
maturity band between one month and three months inclusive.
The liquidity coverage ratio for the sector was 358.5%, ranging
from 187.1% to 792.7%, an increase of 40.5 percentage points
compared to the end of the previous year. The given indicator was
also met by all banks (limit ≥70% – as of 1 January 2021).
Thus, the liquidity indicators reveal the existence of adequate
sources of funding to cover both short-term and long-term liquidity
needs.
Liquid assets amounted to MDL 57,558.1 million as at 31.12.2021,
increasing by MDL 4,932.4 million (9.4%) compared to 31.12.2020.
The largest increase was recorded in net current interbank funds,
which amounted to MDL 4,169.0 million (55.5%) and reached MDL
11,683.4 million, followed by liquid securities, which increased by
MDL 771.9 million (4.7%) to MDL 17,312.8 million.

In the structure of liquid assets in the banking sector, the largest
share was held by deposits with the NBM – 39.5%, followed by
the share of liquid securities – 30.1%, the share of net current
interbank funds – 20.3% and the share of cash in hand and other
monetary values – 10.1%, details are presented in the chart 4.17.
Compared to the end of the previous year, the share of deposits
with the NBM decreased by 3.4 percentage points due to the
decrease in the ratio of required reserves held by banks from
32.0% to 26.0%, the share of liquid securities decreased by 1.3
percentage points and the share of cash – by 1.3 percentage points.
At the same time, the share of net interbank funds increased by
6.0 percentage points.
Market risk sensitivity
The share of balance sheet assets in foreign currency combined
with foreign currency pegged assets in total assets as at
31.12.2021 was 35.8%, which is equal to the share of foreign
currency balance sheet liabilities and foreign currency pegged
bonds in total assets. The equal shares of foreign currency assets
and bonds indicate that the foreign exchange risk is well managed
by the banks and is minimal, respectively.
According to the data provided by banks, as of 31.12.2021, the
foreign placements of banks from the Republic of Moldova
amounted to MDL 12,412.1 million, increasing by MDL 3,394.5
million (37.6%) compared to the end of the previous year and
representing 10.5% of total assets (as of 31.12.2020 – 8.7%) and
81.9% of own funds (as of 31.12.2020 – 66.2%). The greatest
increases were recorded for placements in Germany – by MDL
2,356.4 million (58.3%), France – by MDL 455.2 million (84.1%),
Austria – by MDL 356.5 million (19.2%) and the USA – by MDL
235.9 million (17.1%).
At the same time, placements in Italy
decreased the most – by MDL 119.5 million (14.9%).
Details are shown in Table A.15.


Chapter 4. Banking supervision
75
As at 31.12.2021, the largest share in total placements abroad
went to Germany – 51.5%, followed by Austria – 17.8%, the USA –
13.0%, and France – 8.0%. Placements are predominantly made
in highly rated countries and banks, which indicates a prudent
activity.
According to the Regulation on the classiﬁcation of assets and
contingent liabilities, banks reduce their own funds by the size
of the reductions formed, considering the rating assigned by
international agencies to countries and banks.
4.2
The NBM supervisory activity
To protect the interests of depositors and to ensure the stability
and viability of the banking system, the National Bank of Moldova
carries out prudential supervision of banks in the Republic of
Moldova,
ensuring
compliance
with
legal
requirements
for
preventing and limiting risks speciﬁc to banking activity.
The negative effects of the COVID-19 pandemic triggered in early
2020 were felt in 2021 in most spheres of the economy. Despite
the fact that during 2021 some banking sector indicators showed
positive dynamics, the risk of adverse effects remained.
The unpredictability of the economic effects of the COVID-19
pandemic on banks, as well as risk-based supervision, prompted
the National Bank to review its actions taken in the process of
supervising the activity of banks, with a view to focusing mainly
on those areas where the greatest risks may occur.
The main areas on which the banking supervision process focused
in 2021, according to the priorities set, were: credit risk, risk
associated with information and communication technologies
(ICT), internal governance, risk management and internal capital
adequacy
assessment
process
(ICAAP),
combating
money
laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing, and payment systems.
In 2021, the NBM continued the supervision of banks under the
supervisory review and evaluation process SREP.
Thus, the
Executive Board of the NBM approved the results of the
supervisory review and evaluation process (SREP), with the
imposition of speciﬁc capital requirements to each bank according
to its individual risk proﬁle.
Off-site inspections
The National Bank carries out off-site supervision based on the
annual prudential supervision programme.
For 2021,
the
prudential supervision programme included various ongoing
actions (monitoring of key indicators, analysis of ﬁnancial reports
submitted by banks,
external audit reports,
monitoring of
remediation plans following on-site inspections) as well as actions
for
the
preparation
of
the
SREP
report
(business
model
assessment,
governance
and
internal
control
analysis,
assessment of recovery plans, etc.).
In 2021, the credit risk remained an important area of supervisory
priorities. The NBM reviewed, on its own initiative, each bank’s


76
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
credit portfolio under the Credit Risk Register for supervision and
identiﬁcation of imprudent practices.
In the case of credit
classiﬁcation contrary to the regulations in force, the NBM
requested the removal of the violations detected.
The National Bank will continue to act in accordance with the
legislation in force and international best practices, applying all
the instruments assigned by law and statute, to increase the level
of transparency of the ownership structure and ensure prudent
and sound management of licensed banks.
On-site inspections
During 2021,
7 planned targeted on-site inspections were
conducted
at
licensed
banks.
To
remove
violations
and
deﬁciencies found during the inspections, supervisory measures
were ordered against two banks and one bank was sanctioned
with a warning.
Overall, the breaches and deﬁciencies identiﬁed during the
targeted inspections relate to credit risk management, internal
governance and internal control functions, ICT risk, and payment
systems business area.
Decisions imposing sanctions and prescriptions were adopted after
ﬁnding of breaches and deﬁciencies mainly related to the following:
• failure to comply with certain requirements of the legislation
on lending activity, including the classiﬁcation of assets and
contingent liabilities;
• non-compliance with reporting requirements to the National
Bank of Moldova;
• lack of a rigorous framework for stress testing;
• failure to fully implement all the measures set out in the
Action Plan on the cessation and liquidation of breaches and
other shortcomings identiﬁed after the previous inspection;
• non-compliance with the requirements of regulatory acts and
other deﬁciencies, which are related to corporate governance
in the bank, including internal control functions.
Control of foreign exchange entities
According to art. 57 par. (3) letter a1 and art. 62 par. (1) of Law
No.62/2008 on foreign exchange regulation, the NBM shall control
the compliance of foreign exchange entities with the provisions of
the foreign exchange legislation.
During 2021, 23 planned and 2 unannounced controls on the
activity of foreign exchange entities were carried out, following
which violations were found such as:
• reporting with errors/non-reporting/non-compliance with the
frequency of changes in foreign exchange rates;
• divergences between the rate set by order and the one
displayed on the board or the one applied de facto, incorrect
recording of rate orders;


Chapter 4. Banking supervision
77
• use
of
forms
not
updated
in
accordance
with
legal
requirements;
• failure to display the required information in a visible place
for individuals;
• failure to notify the NBM of changes in the data submitted for
licensing;
• failure to ensure the availability/maintenance of circulating
funds at the level established by law;
• lack of banknote authenticity veriﬁcation machine;
• submission of non-authentic data to the NBM.
As a result of the violations and deﬁciencies found during the on-
site inspections carried out at the foreign exchange entities, the
following decisions on the application of sanctions were adopted,
namely:
• 1 decision of the Executive Board ordering the withdrawal of
the license of the foreign exchange entity;
• 5 decisions of the Executive Board ordering the suspension
of the foreign exchange entity’s activity;
• 10 decisions of the Executive Board imposing a ﬁne on the
foreign exchange entity;
• 8 written warnings.
Early
intervention
and
transparency
actions
on
shareholdings
As at 31.12.2021, B.C. "ENERGBANK" S.A. is supervised under
the early intervention regime applied on 11.01.2019, due to the
ﬁnding of concerted activity of a group of shareholders with a
qualiﬁed holding in the amount of 52.55% in the bank’s capital,
without the prior written permission of the NBM, the effects of
the law on the suspension of the exercise of certain rights of
shareholders becoming applicable. Subsequently, by Decisions
of the Executive Board of the NBM, the period for appointing
temporary administrators of B.C."ENERGBANK" S.A. was extended
until 29.04.2022.
During 2021,
no signiﬁcant changes were recorded in the
shareholding structure of licensed banks in the Republic of
Moldova.


78
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
Banks in liquidation
As of 31.12.2021, 4 banks were in the process of forced liquidation.
As to the liquidation process of Banca de Economii S.A., BC "BANCA
SOCIAL˘A" S.A. and BC "UNIBANK" S.A., initiated in 2015, we note
that, with the aim of recovering the funds of these banks, during
2021, the liquidators reported that they cooperated with the
prosecution authorities, undertook organizational measures for the
sale of assets owned and possessed by the banks and continued
working with the banks’ debtors.
In its activity, the bank liquidator has full and exclusive powers to
direct, manage and control the bank and to take any measures
with a view to its efﬁcient liquidation and obtaining the maximum
amount from the sale of assets.
As a result, at the end of 2021, 49 actions were pending in the
courts for the recovery of funds previously granted by banks, for
a total amount of MDL 3,934.1 million. At the same time, there
were 72 criminal cases under the management of the prosecution
authorities, ﬁled based on liquidators’ referrals.
As a result of the actions undertaken by the liquidators during the
liquidation process of the three banks, funds in the total amount of
MDL 2,745.7 million were collected, including in 2021 – MDL 144.8
million (Banca de Economii S.A. – MDL 53.3 million, BC "BANCA
SOCIAL˘A" S.A. – MDL 66.4 million and B.C. "UNIBANK" S.A. – MDL
25.1 million).
The funds obtained in the bank liquidation process were used for
expenses related to the liquidation, as well as for the payment of
claims related to emergency loans granted to Banca de Economii
S.A., BC "BANCA SOCIAL˘A" S.A., and B.C. "UNIBANK" S.A., before
the appointment of the liquidators of the respective banks. Thus,
by 31.12.2021, funds in the amount of MDL 2,665.9 million were
repaid, including in 2021 – MDL 108.5 million (Banca de Economii
S.A. – MDL 44.0 million, BC "BANCA SOCIAL˘A" S.A. – MDL 51.0
million and B.C. "UNIBANK" S.A. – MDL 13.5 million).
Mechanisms of resolution ﬁnancing
In 2020, the Bank Resolution Fund became operational. It was set
up to cover the needs related to the effective application of
resolution tools in accordance with the provisions of Law
No.232/2016 on bank recovery and resolution.
The Bank Resolution Fund consists of:
- annual contributions to reach the target level set in the law;
- extraordinary contributions, in case the annual contributions
are insufﬁcient in the opinion of the resolution authority;
- loans and other forms of support.


Chapter 4. Banking supervision
79
The resources of the Bank Resolution Fund are administered by
the Deposit Guarantee Fund in the Banking System, which, as
administrator of the Bank Resolution Fund, is obliged to invest the
available ﬁnancial resources of the Fund in low-risk assets. The
use of the resources of the Bank Resolution Fund is decided by the
NBM, as resolution authority, for the purposes described in Law
No.232/2016, and the calculation and payment of contributions
by banks is carried out in accordance with the Regulation on the
method of calculation and payment of contributions to the Bank
Resolution Fund, approved on 30.04.2020.
According to the law, the ﬁnancial resources of the Bank Resolution
Fund shall reach 3.0% of the amount of guaranteed deposits of all
banks licensed on the territory of the Republic of Moldova. At the
time of the last calculation of the annual contributions for 2022,
this amount was about MDL 515.5 million. As of 31.12.2021, banks
have fully paid the annual contributions calculated by the NBM for
2020 and 2021. Thus, at the end of 2021, the resources of the
Resolution Fund constituted MDL 186.8 million.
Also, the National Bank notiﬁed the licensed banks about the
amounts, tranches, deadlines and form of payment of contributions
to the Bank Resolution Fund, to be paid by the end of 2022. Thus,
the calculated annual contributions to be paid during 2022 by all
banks is MDL 146.3 million.
The
establishment
of
the
Resolution
Fund
contributes
to
strengthening the banking sector and its credibility, in support of
depositors’ interests.
4.3
Banking sector risks’ assessment
Macroprudential supervision
During
2021,
the
National
Bank
of
Moldova
promoted
macroprudential supervision determined by the developments in
the domestic ﬁnancial sector, in the macroeconomic context of
the Republic of Moldova and the countries in the region, as well as
in the context of the COVID-19 epidemiological situation.
In order not to allow excessive accumulation of systemic risk and
to ensure the stability of the banking sector in the adverse
conditions caused by the COVID-19 epidemiological situation,
during 2021, the National Bank of Moldova continued to conduct
regular stress tests and sensitivity studies of the banking sector.
These
macroprudential
analysis
tools
contributed
to
the
assessment of the resilience of Moldovan banks to potential
shocks
generated
by
the
uncertainty
of
macroeconomic
developments during the reporting period.
The stress tests used by the National Bank focus on credit risk,
liquidity risk, interest rate risk, foreign exchange rate risk and
contagion risk scenarios and combinations thereof. The stress
tests are based on information reported by banks and estimate
the impact of simulated shocks at the banking sector level and at
the level of individual banks.


80
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
The results of the credit risk stress tests conducted at the banking
sector level do not indicate the build-up of signiﬁcant systemic
vulnerabilities.
At the same time, the simulation of shocks at
the level of individual banks indicates that banks’ own funds
are sufﬁcient and most of the simulated shocks did not lead to
breaches of the minimum prudential capital levels set for banks.
In 2021, the results of the stress tests conﬁrmed the high level of
liquidity of the banking sector, with banks being able to withstand
scenarios simulating deposit withdrawals at historical highs and
no liquidity shortfalls or breaches of prudential requirements for
the stress scenarios applied.
Also, to assess potential risks that could affect the stability of the
sector and of individual banks, the National Bank conducts the
study on the identiﬁcation of banking sector vulnerabilities. During
2021, the results of the study indicated a favourable evolution
of the banking sector vulnerability. At the individual level, most
banks continued the process of strengthening and improving their
resilience. Although some banks have experienced insigniﬁcant
build-ups of vulnerabilities for some balance sheet items, they
remain below the alert threshold.
To survey the opinion of risk managers in banks, the National Bank
of Moldova conducts the systemic risk survey every six months.
According to the respondents’ opinion, both traditional key risks
(macroeconomic) and speciﬁc risks (COVID-19, geopolitical) persist
in the ﬁnancial system. At the same time, at the end of 2021, banks
estimated at medium to high level the probability of occurrence of
an event with high impact on the ﬁnancial system in the next 12
months. The medium-term (1-3 years) expectations of an adverse
event are also estimated at medium to high level.
To determine the ﬁnancing conditions and risk proﬁle of the sectors
ﬁnanced by banks in the Republic of Moldova, the NBM conducts
the quarterly bank lending survey. At the end of 2021, the results
of the survey indicated a relaxation of lending standards for legal
entities and a tightening of standards for individuals. Banks’ short-
term expectations were for a slight easing of lending standards for
legal entities and a tightening of lending standards for individuals.
Macroprudential policy tools
To
increase
the
resilience
of
banks
to
possible
adverse
developments, within the strategic objective of strengthening the
ﬁnancial stability function and macroprudential supervision, the
National Bank of Moldova, as the supervisory authority, applies
the macroprudential supervisory toolkit for banks in the Republic
of Moldova.
Thus, the Executive Board of the National Bank of Moldova set the
capital buffer rates applied to the banks of the Republic of Moldova
as of the end of 2021 as follows:
• The capital conservation buffer rate applied to all banks was
set at the level of 2.5% of the total amount of banks’ risk
exposure. In December 2021, the NBM decided to revoke the
temporary measures related to the capital conservation
buffer requirements for banks introduced in April 2020,
which allowed banks to use the previously built-up capital
conservation buffer, and to revert to the pre-pandemic
capital conservation buffer requirements.


Chapter 4. Banking supervision
81
• The countercyclical buffer rate for exposures located in the
Republic of Moldova has been maintained at 0% of the
amount of banks’ risk exposure.
• The systemic risk buffer rate for exposures located in the
Republic of Moldova was maintained at the level of 1.0% or
3.0% of the banks’ risk exposure amount, depending on the
ownership structure of the respective bank.
• To ensure an adequate treatment of structural risks of the
banking sector in the Republic of Moldova, the National Bank
determines the banks that are systemically important
institutions (O-SIIs). Based on the results of the study on the
identiﬁcation of banks that are O-SIIs and in accordance with
paragraph 53 of Regulation No. 110/2018 on capital buffers
of banks, the National Bank has established the capital buffer
rates for systemically important institutions for banks
identiﬁed as O-SIIs in the Republic of Moldova as follows:
1. 1.5% for BC "MOLDOVA-AGROINDBANK" S.A.,
2. 1.0% for BC "Moldindconbank" S.A.,
3. 1.0% for BC "VICTORIABANK" S.A.,
4. 0.5% for OTP Bank S.A.
Therefore, at the end of 2021 the total capital buffer requirement
applied to banks in the Republic of Moldova varied between 3.5%
and 7.0%, depending on the ownership structure and systemic
importance of the bank.
4.4
Combating money laundering and
terrorist ﬁnancing
The National Bank of Moldova ensures the supervision of the
application of the regulatory framework related to the prevention
and combating of money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing, in
accordance with the provisions of the Law No.
308/2017 on
the prevention and combating of money laundering and terrorist
ﬁnancing, for the following reporting entities: banks, payment
institutions, electronic money issuers and postal service providers
operating in accordance with the Law No. 114/2012 on payment
services and electronic money and foreign exchange entities.
In 2021, the National Bank of Moldova continued to improve its risk-
based supervisory processes considering the money laundering
and terrorist ﬁnancing risk to which the entities within its area
of responsibility are exposed.
In this context, the Guidelines
on risk-based approach to prevent and ﬁght money laundering
and terrorist ﬁnancing was developed and approved, which is a
policy document describing the mechanism for identifying and
assessing money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing risks in the
sectors supervised by the NBM, as well as how to effectively apply
risk-based supervision.
At the same time, in the speciﬁc epidemiological context of the
reference period, the risk-based assessment and supervision
process has been continuously adjusted according to the exposure


82
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
of supervised entities to money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing
risk. Thus, during 2021, the supervision activity of entities under
the NBM’s area of responsibility included a number of off-site
inspections, the objective of which was both the assessment of
internal
control
programmes
and
systems
at
the
level
of
supervised entities, dedicated to PCMLTF, and the veriﬁcation of
higher risk operations, such as external transfers, operations
carried out through card payment systems and digital applications
(more than 35 veriﬁcations carried out remotely), etc. In this way,
21 inspections were carried out in 2021, including 2 controls on
banks, 2 controls on non-bank payment service providers and 17
controls on foreign exchange entities (other than banks). As a
result of the inspections carried out, in cases of identiﬁcation of
non-compliance with the provisions of the legislation in the ﬁeld of
prevention of money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing, sanctions
and/or measures to remedy the deﬁciencies were ordered, in
accordance with the legislation in force, including the provisions
of Law No.
75/2020 on the procedure for the detection of
violations in the ﬁeld of prevention of money laundering and
terrorist ﬁnancing and the manner of application of sanctions. At
the same time, each reporting entity has set and implemented,
through its own action plans,
actions aimed at removing
deﬁciencies in internal control systems and strengthening the
compliance function in the area of reference.
The processes and proﬁle checks carried out under the supervision
of the given sector also included continuous information ﬂows
between the National Bank and the supervised entities, the amount
and intensity of which was determined by the type of supervised
entity, business model, internal governance and, of course, the
level of money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing risk speciﬁc to
the supervised segment.
In the ﬁeld of regulation of prevention and combating money
laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing and in accordance with the
recommendations of the MONEYVAL Committee (Council of
Europe), recorded in the Evaluation Report on the National system
for preventing and combating money laundering and terrorist
ﬁnancing of the Republic of Moldova adopted during the 5th round
of
evaluation
in
July
201937,
during
2021,
a
number
of
amendments to the Regulations related to the requirements on
preventing
and
combating
money
laundering
and
terrorist
ﬁnancing in the activity of banks, non-bank payment service
providers, foreign exchange ofﬁces and hotels were approved
(Regulation No. 200/2018, Regulation No. 201/2018, Regulation
No. 202/2018). The approved amendments concerned mandatory
customer due diligence measures, requirements on data retention,
on the execution of due diligence measures by third party entities,
internal control system and programme speciﬁc requirements at
the level of ﬁnancial groups.
In 2021, the NBM in collaboration with its external partners
continued
the
process
of
implementing
the
information
technology solution on monitoring shareholder transparency in
banks and remote analysis in the ﬁeld of preventing and
combating money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing.
The
implementation of the information technology solution will enable
the strengthening of the NBM’s operational capacity of remote
supervision of banks, from the perspective of monitoring the risks
37https://rm.coe.int/moneyval-2019-6-5th-round-mer-repmoldova/168097a396


Chapter 4. Banking supervision
83
associated with their activity, including with the use of highly
effective qualitative alert indices on banks’ compliance with the
requirements in force for preventing and combating money
laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing.
As the effectiveness of measures to prevent and ﬁght money
laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing is also determined by the
existence
of
a
multi-institutional
approach,
to
ensure
the
appropriate contribution to Moldova’s overall effort to ﬁght
against economic crime, the National Bank of Moldova has
supported a continuous exchange of information on money
laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing risks with other supervisory
authorities in the country, as well as with international bodies with
relevant expertise. In this way, in 2021 the NBM continued to
contribute to the national risk assessment process by assessing
and submitting the relevant information to the competent
authority responsible for the national money laundering and
terrorist ﬁnancing risk assessment process.
In the same vein, in 2021 the National Bank actively participated
in the activities related to the assessment of the Global Forum
on Transparency and Exchange of Information for Tax Purposes
(OECD), in particular with regard to supervisory actions in the
banking sector and the implementation by reporting entities of the
requirements related to the prevention and ﬁght against money
laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing.
The NBM’s efforts to strengthen the capacity of the supervised
sector to effectively manage the characteristic money laundering
and
terrorist
ﬁnancing
risks
included
the
organisation
or
participation of the authority as co-organiser of events dedicated
to prevention and combating money laundering and terrorist
ﬁnancing. Thus, in 2021, trainings, workshops and conferences
were held dedicated to the identiﬁcation and assessment of risks
associated with the ﬁnancial sector, typologies of operations,
which
may
be
circumscribed
to
suspected
money
laundering/terrorist
ﬁnancing
operations,
prevention
of
proliferation of weapons of mass destruction, etc.


84
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
Chapter 5
Financial market infrastructures
5.1
Regulation
and
supervision
of
ﬁnancial market infrastructures
In accordance with Law No. 548/1995 on the National Bank of
Moldova, the regulation and supervision of ﬁnancial market
infrastructures in the Republic of Moldova is one of the core tasks
of the National Bank.
This task is implemented according to the best international
practices, recommendations, principles, and standards in this
domain issued by the Bank for International Settlements, the
European Central Bank, the International Monetary Fund, and the
World Bank.
To modernize the automated interbank payment system (AIPS) of
the NBM and implement the instant payment scheme, provided for
in the Concept for the development of the payment sector in the
Republic of Moldova, two implementation projects were started in
2021.
These projects aim at developing the payment system in the
Republic of Moldova and modernizing payment services, to provide
the ﬁnancial community in our country a payment system with
high-performance infrastructure in line with the expectations and
demands of payment service providers, but also modern payment
tools meeting the needs of individual and corporate consumers.
The ﬁrst implementation project is dedicated to the modernization
of AIPS and includes the logical separation of AIPS modules RTGS
(real-time gross settlement system) and DNS (net settlement
clearing system), the transition to a more advanced payment
message format ISO 20022), the creation of a functional model
for the management of liquidity limits by AIPS participants. The
modernization work was carried out during 2021 without deviations
from the deadlines set out in the project plan, thus the launch of
the new system is planned for May 2022.
The second project is dedicated to the implementation of a new
payment scheme (instant payments). During the year, activities
related to the procurement procedure of the software solution were
carried out: the public tender was organized, the submitted offers
were evaluated, and the winning supplier was designated.
The measures concerned will strengthen the payment system in
the Republic of Moldova, improve the management of operational
and liquidity risks, and facilitate the implementation of new
payment tools.
In addition, premises will be created for the


Chapter 5. Financial market infrastructures
85
efﬁcient development of innovative payments, including mobile
ones,
with
immediate
ﬁnal
settlement
and
possibility
of
immediate reuse of received funds.
As a result, the circuit of
economic ﬂows will become easier; the innovative payments
sector will have a better ability to attract investment and will
increase the velocity of money in the economy. The measures will
bring beneﬁts at multiple levels – to payment service providers
and ﬁnal consumers (individuals), as well as economic operators
and authorities.
During the third quarter of 2021, a workshop was organized jointly
with the Ministry of Foreign Affairs and European Integration of
the Republic of Moldova and representatives of the European
Payments Council on the inclusion of the Republic of Moldova in the
geographical scope of the SEPA, to address strategic issues for the
purpose of the accession of the banking and non-banking payment
service providers of the Republic of Moldova to the SEPA schemes,
as well as other issues related to the criteria for extending the
geographical scope of the SEPA.
According to Article 5 of Law No. 548/1995 on the National Bank
of Moldova, the NBM supervises ﬁnancial market infrastructures,
under the conditions established by law, and promotes their stable
and efﬁcient functioning. In the context of the primary objective,
the National Bank supervises the payment and settlement systems
in the Republic of Moldova, which stable and efﬁcient functioning is
essential for ﬁnancial stability, implementation of monetary policy,
and promotion of public conﬁdence in making cashless payments.
In the domain of payments and settlement systems’ supervision,
the following are subject to supervision:
1. Payment and settlement systems (high value payment
systems and high volume and low value payment systems);
2. Clearing and settlement systems of securities;
3. Money remittances;
4. Payment tools;
5. Automated remote service systems.
When carrying out its oversight function, the NBM pays special
attention to systems that process, compensate, and settle high-
value payments and pose a substantial risk in the event of an
operational or settlement failure.
At the same time, the NBM
ensures an appropriate level of attention to all components of the
payment system subject to supervision.
5.2
Licensing,
regulation
and
supervision
of
the
activity
of
providing payment and electronic
money issuing services
According to Law No.
114/2012 on payment services and
electronic money, the NBM licenses, regulates, and supervises


86
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
the activity of payment service providers and electronic money
issuers.
The licensing process of non-bank payment service providers
(payment companies, electronic money issuers and postal service
providers) and that of registration of payment agents is carried
out in compliance with a set of requirements, which ensure a
uniform and competitive approach to access to the activity of
providing payment services and its performance on the territory
of the Republic of Moldova.
As of 31.12.2021, seven non-bank payment service providers
were operating in the Republic of Moldova, including one payment
company, one postal service provider, and ﬁve electronic money
issuing companies.
The regulatory framework developed by the NBM in the context
of the activity of providing payment services aligns the relevant
internal regulations with the European rules, materializing in the
adoption of the normative acts necessary to carry out the activity
on the payment services market. This framework is an essential
support for increasing the number of modern and secure payment
services, increasing competition in the payment services market
due to the non-banking providers’ access to the market, thus
promoting better efﬁciency and quality, as well as achieving a high
level of non-cash payments and reduction of cash in circulation.
In 2021, the National Bank of Moldova, jointly with the Ministry of
Finance, went through a number of stages in the process of
legislating the Draft Law No. 199/MF/NBM/2020, which aims to
transpose into national law the provisions of Directive (EU)
2015/2366 on payment services in the internal market (according
to international usage – PSD2), such as draft law consultation with
the competent authorities to issue the appropriate expertise,
examination of the received expertise, and meetings with all
authorities participating in the expertise, to address various
aspects of the draft. Also, at the end of the year, the draft law was
ﬁnalized for further promotion by the Government for approval
and subsequently submitted to the Parliament of the Republic of
Moldova.
The transposition of the European Directive (PSD2) into national
legislation is an exercise to further align the regulatory framework
for payment services with the new EU regulations in the ﬁeld and
an important act for innovations in payment services, following
the best practices and experiences in the region.
Among the main amendments of the draft law transposing PSD2,
we can list the following: new payment methods and solutions,
more legal clarity of the current legal framework on payment
services, setting new rules for both existing and new payment
service providers and, ﬁnally, more protection for payment service
users.
Also, on 26.03.2021, the Draft Executive Board Decision No.
217/2019 amending the Regulation on the activity of non-bank
payment service providers was subjected to public consultation, in
the context of the need to optimize the regulatory framework
applicable to the activity of non-bank payment service providers,
with
the
adoption
and
improvement
of
the
rules
for
licensing/registration through the Information System of the


Chapter 5. Financial market infrastructures
87
National Bank of Moldova on licensing,
authorization,
and
notiﬁcation, registration of agents in the Register of payment
institutions/postal service providers/Register of electronic money
institutions and efﬁcient capital management.
In accordance with Art.
93(2)(b) of Law No.
114/2012, the
National Bank supervises the activity of payment companies,
electronic money issuing companies, postal service providers as
payment service providers, banks as payment service providers
and electronic money issuers.
In 2021, targeted inspections were carried out at six licensed
banks, during which the banks’ compliance with payment systems
legislation was veriﬁed.
Also, during 2021, four targeted inspections and one complex
off-site inspection were carried out at licensed non-bank payment
service providers, during which their activity was subject to
veriﬁcation.
As a result of the inspections carried out,
proposals and
recommendations were submitted on the actions to be taken to
remove the violations and shortcomings detected and, where
appropriate, remedial measures were ordered, and sanctions were
applied.

The NBM also carried out off-site monitoring of the activity of

through non-bank payment service providers, decreasing by 7.7%
compared to 2020, in the total amount of MDL 29.3 billion, 8.9%
more than in 2020 (Chart 5.1).
5.3
Automated
interbank
payment
system
The automated interbank payment system (AIPS) is the system
through which payments are made automatically in Moldovan lei
of/through participants on the territory of the Republic of Moldova.
The AIPS consists of the real-time gross settlement system (the
RTGS system) and the designated-time net settlement system
(DNS system). The RTGS system is designed to process urgent and
high-value payments, and the DNS system is designed to process
small-value payments.
At the end of 2021, 20 participants were registered in AIPS, namely:
• The National Bank of Moldova;
• 11 banks licensed by the National Bank of Moldova;
• Cash and settlement centre in Tiraspol city;


88
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
• 3 non-bank payment service providers;
• The State Treasury within the Ministry of Finance, including
as a mandated participant;
• The Single Central Securities Depository (CSD);
• Deposit Guarantee Fund in Banking System.
In 2021, a total volume of 13.8 million transactions worth MDL 1.1
trillion were processed through AIPS, increasing both in number
(9.8%) and value (0.7%) compared to 2020. The average daily
volume38 of transactions amounted to 54.6 thousand transactions
worth MDL 4.3 billion.
The maximum volume of daily settled
payments in AIPS was reached on 25 October 2021, with 103.0
thousand payments settled.

On average, 5.9 thousand payments amounting to MDL 3.9 billion
were settled through the RTGS system daily, while through the DNS
system – 48.6 thousand payments amounting to MDL 397.1 million.
The average value of a payment settled in the RTGS system was
MDL 656.0 thousand, and of a payment settled in the DNS system
– MDL 8.2 thousand (Table A.16).
As to the structure of payments made through AIPS in 2021, it
was found that payment transactions made by participants on
behalf of customers prevailed (99.9% of the number of settled
transactions), but their total value remains relatively low (only
40.0% of the value of settled transactions).
Considering the structure of payments made on behalf of clients
by AIPS participants, we ﬁnd the following representation:
• ordinary customer transfers – 57.9% of total payments;
• transfers related to budget payments – 39.2%;
• other types of payments – 2.9%.
In terms of value, transactions in AIPS, made on behalf of clients
have the following structure:
• ordinary customer transfers – 64.5% of total payments;
• budget transfers – 27.6%;
• other types of payments – 7.9%.
During 2021, the average availability of the system for participants
was 99.9%, which illustrates the extent to which AIPS participants
had the ability to use AIPS without discontinuities or incidents,
during the operating program.

Chapter 5. Financial market infrastructures

5.4
The
Single
Central
Securities
Depository
The
Single
Central
Securities
Depository
(CSD)
has
been
operational since 31 July 2018 and is administered (managed and
operated) by the Single Central Securities Depository JSC.
The CSD system is a securities settlement system, which ensures
the provision by the Central Depository of securities registration,
centralized administration, settlement and, where appropriate,
ancillary services. The CSD system is based on a modern direct
processing technology, connected to AIPS, and provides an
efﬁcient
infrastructure
and
services
for
the
settlement
of
securities transactions and registration of ownership of securities.
At the end of 2021, 20 participants were registered within the CSD
system, including:
• The National Bank of Moldova;
• 11 banks licensed by the National Bank of Moldova;
• Deposit Guarantee Fund in the Banking System;
• 7 non-bank investment companies.
Securities registration services provided by CSD
At the end of 2021, 336.5 million SS and NBC securities were
registered in the CSD system at a nominal value of MDL 37.0
billion.
At the same time, according to the data presented by CSD at
the end of 2021, 897.4 million corporate securities (CS) were
registered in the CSD system, of which 895.1 million ordinary
shares, 2.3 million preferential shares, and 7.5 thousand municipal
bonds. The total value of CS constituted MDL 12.1 billion for the
reference period, of which 98.6% of the total value of CS were
ordinary shares.
Settlement services
During 2021, 6.3 thousand transfer orders worth MDL 313.3 billion
were settled in the CSD system.
Supervision of CSD activity
During the reporting year, an off-site targeted inspection was
carried out on the activity of the CSD, during which the measures
taken by the CSD to remedy the shortcomings identiﬁed by the
NBM previously, following the complex on-site inspection carried
out in 2020, were subject to veriﬁcation.


90
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
5.5
Cashless payment tools
Cashless payment tools are essential components of the payment
system and the National Bank conducts their supervision to ensure
efﬁciency and security of their use.
Out of payment tools available, the most used by population
(individuals) are payment cards. It is worth mentioning that the
NBM is undertaking relevant measures together with other national
authorities and institutions to promote the use of cashless payment
tools and make the circulation of money transparent.
Payment cards
Number of cards39 in circulation at the end of 2021 constituted
2.35 million units and increased by 7.4% compared to end of 2020
(Chart 5.3).
On the background of the increase in the number of cards in
circulation, the number of cards which are active40 had an increase
by 11.9% compared to the end of 2020 and the share of active
cards in the total number of cards in circulation did not change
signiﬁcantly, accounting for 63.0%. It is worth mentioning that this
rate of card usage has been virtually constant over the last few
years.

Concerning the technical solution of payment cards, the number
of proximity (contactless) cards registered an increase of 24.6%
compared to 2020. The rapid increase in the share of these cards
in the total number of cards held contributed to the gradual
replacement of hybrid cards,
which in turn had previously
substituted the magnetic stripe cards. Thus, at the end of 2021
contactless cards held the major share (92.7%) of the total cards
in
circulation
in
the
Republic
of
Moldova.
Hybrid
cards
(microprocessor card with magnetic stripe) the number of which
fell by 60.4% compared to the end of 2020, account for 7.2% of
the total cards in circulation in the Republic of Moldova. Magnetic
tape cards have an ever-insigniﬁcant share, accounting for only
0.02% of the total number of cards in circulation, even though at
the end of 2014 these cards had the most signiﬁcant share
(55.1%). Virtual cards have a modest share of only 0.1%, with no
signiﬁcant variation over the last few years.
During 2021, through payment cards issued by payment service
providers in the Republic of Moldova, 129.5 million transactions
amounting to MDL 98.8 billion were carried out both in the country
and abroad, increasing by 34.9% in number and 30.6% in value
compared to 2020.
Out of the total volume of transactions performed with cards
issued in the country, 78.4% represented cashless payments, and

Automated remote service systems
Automated
remote
services
systems
(ARSS)
are
computer
solutions and/or equipment made available to the holders by the
payment service providers, which enable them through an IT
application, authentication method and means of communication
to have remote access to funds on the payment account for the
purpose of obtaining information about the status of the payment
account and transactions made, carrying out transactions on
behalf of the holder from the funds on the payment account.
The total number of ARSS holders amounted to 2,572.6 thousand
at the end of 2021, increasing by 23.3% compared to 2020. The
number of active holders43 also showed an upward trend of 25.7%,
reaching 787.8 thousand, indicating the growing interest and
conﬁdence of payment service providers’ customers to use ARSS.
Out of the total number of ARSS holders, 30.6% are active ARSS
holders (Chart 5.8).
The distribution of ARSS holders by type of systems used reveals
that 56.5% of them are registered in internet-payments systems
(0.5 percentage points more than at the end of 2020); 43.4% of
their total number are mobile-payments holders (2.3 percentage

93
points more than at the end of 2020); 0.06% use PC-payments (PC-
payments holders are only legal entities), this share decreasing
by 0.01 percentage points compared to the end of 2020. At the
same time, by the end of 2021, payment service providers stopped
offering telephone-payments systems.
In 2021, through the ARSS, 39.0 million transactions were carried
out, 32.2% more than in 2020. The increase in the number of
transactions through the ARSS can be explained by the fact that
customers of payment service providers are increasingly paying for
various services/goods through the ARSS, due to the convenience
it offers, a phenomenon that became more signiﬁcant during the
COVID-19 pandemic.
The value of all transactions made through ARSS by individuals
and legal entities amounted to MDL 769.3 billion during 2021, an
increase of 25.5% compared to the previous year, which indicates
an increase in customer conﬁdence to conduct transactions
through ARSS (Chart 5.9).
About 96.5% of the value of
transactions conducted through the ARSS were made by legal
entities.

Out of the total number of credit transfer payment transactions
initiated by bank customers (20.8 million transactions), 88.3%

Also, in relative terms, the number of credit transfers initiated

Direct debiting
During 2021, through direct debiting, 193.9 thousand transactions
were carried out for a total amount of MDL 151.1 million, increasing
in number by 13.6% and in value by 29.6% compared to 2020.
The total number of users of this tool at the end of 2021 was about
9.2 thousand, 72.6% more than in the previous year.

In 2021, the value of currency in circulation continued its upward
trend, with a growth rate (+5.1%, up to a value of MDL 34,761.0

6.1
Evolution of currency (banknotes
and coins in circulation)
Banknotes in circulation at the end of 2021, in terms of value,
amounted to MDL 34,494.9 million, about 5.0% more than at the
end of 2020. The evolution in value of banknotes in circulation is
shown in the Chart 6.1.
At the end of 2021 there were around 317.9 million banknotes in
circulation, 4.2% less than at the end of 2020. The evolution of
banknotes in circulation, in terms of quantity, by each nominal
value is shown in Chart 6.2.
Banknotes with a nominal value of MDL 1 continue to account for
a major share of 26.3% in the number of banknotes in circulation,
followed by banknotes with a nominal value of MDL 200, with
a share of 25.6%, and MDL 100, with a share of 15.6%.
The
smallest share in the total number of banknotes in circulation went
to banknotes with a face value of MDL 500 (3.7%) and MDL 1,000
(1.5%).
According to the nominal value structure, at the end of the
reporting period, the quantity of banknotes in circulation was
approximately: for 1 leu - 83.5 million banknotes, 5 lei – 15.5
million banknotes, 10 lei – 17.8 million banknotes, 20 lei – 13.5
million banknotes, 50 lei – 40.0 million banknotes, 100 lei – 49.5
million banknotes, 200 lei – 81.5 million banknotes, 500 lei – 11.8
million banknotes and 1,000 lei – 4.8 million banknotes.
The nominal value structure of banknotes in circulation at the end
of 2021 is represented in terms of quantity and value in the Chart
6.3.
At the end of 2021, there were 912.8 million divisional coins in
circulation, amounting to MDL 133.1 million, with the quantitative
value being 3.1% and the value 3.4% higher than in 2020 (Charts
6.4 and 6.5).
The increase in the quantity of coins in circulation results from
the predominant increase in the number of 5 bani, 10 bani and
25 bani nominal coins. According to the face value structure in
terms of quantity, at the end of the reference period, 1 ban coins

had a share of 7.8%, 5 bani – 25.9%, 10 bani – 30.8%, 25 bani
– 30.5% and 50 bani – 5.0%, and in terms of value 0.5%, 8.9%,
21.1%, 52.5% and 17.0% respectively (Chart 6.6).
At the end of 2021, there were MDL 57.7 million coins in circulation,
1.5 times more than in 2020 (+52.7%), their value accounting for
MDL 116.3 million – 1.8 times more than in the previous year
(+81.6%) (Charts 6.7 and 6.8).
The quantitative increase of metallic MDL coins in circulation
results from the predominant increase in the number of coins
with a face value of MDL 1, MDL 2, and MDL 5. According to the
structure by nominal value in quantitative terms, at the end of
the reference period, MDL 1 coins had a share of 55.3%, MDL 2
coins – 31.0%, MDL 5 coins – 10.6% and MDL 10 coins, including
commemorative coins – 3.1%, and in terms of value, 27.4%, 30.8%,
26.2% and 15.6% respectively (Chart 6.9).

Banknotes issued in 2021, in value terms, amounted to MDL
5,410.4 million (of which MDL 3,860.6 million are modernized
banknotes), about 44.0% less than in 2020, and in quantity terms
– 43.6 million banknotes, about 52.4% less than in 2020.
Decreasing quantities were recorded for MDL 5 and MDL 10
banknotes, due to parallel circulation with coins of the same
denominations, as well as for MDL 20, MDL 50, MDL 100 and MDL
200 banknotes, based on reduced demand on the interbank
market. According to the structure by denomination in terms of
quantity, the banknotes put into circulation were: MDL 5 – 0.5
million banknotes, MDL 10 – 6.9 million banknotes, MDL 20 – 7.5
million banknotes, MDL 50 – 11.7 million banknotes, MDL 100 –
6.1 million banknotes, MDL 200 – 7.4 million banknotes, MDL 500
– 2.1 million banknotes and MDL 1,000 – 1.4 million banknotes.
The nominal value structure of banknotes put into circulation is
shown, in terms of quantity and value, in Chart 6.10 and Chart
6.11 respectively.
In terms of value, the metal divisional coins issued in 2021
amounted to MDL 4.4 million, about 26.5% more than in 2020,
and in terms of quantity – 27.1 million coins (+34.5%).
From a quantitative point of view, the structure of divisional coins
changed compared to 2020 and was: for 5 bani – 4.3 million coins,
10 bani – 10.2 million coins, 25 bani – 12.6 million coins and 50
bani – 0.03 million coins.
The weighting per denomination of the divisional coins issued is
shown, in terms of quantity and value, in Chart 6.12 and Chart
6.13 respectively.
In terms of value, metal MDL coins, including commemorative
coins, issued in 2021 amounted to MDL 52.2 million, about 94.6%
more than in 2020, and in terms of quantity – 19.9 million coins
(+34.4%).
The quantitative structure by face value of metal MDL coins issued
in 2021 is: for MDL 1 – 8.9 million coins, MDL 2 – 5.3 million coins,
MDL 5 – 4.8 million coins, MDL 10 – 0.7 million coins, and for
commemorative coins – 0.2 million coins.
The share of each nominal value of MDL coins issued is shown, in
terms of quantity and value, in the Chart 6.14 and in the Chart
6.17 respectively.

into circulation as a means of payment on the territory of the

The National Bank of Moldova was constantly concerned with
maintaining the quality of banknotes in circulation, in accordance
with the criteria set by the NBM, i.e., in 2021, 61.8 million
banknotes were processed in the central bank, by checking both
authenticity and quality. Following processing, about 32.4 million
banknotes were sorted as inappropriate for circulation.
National currency counterfeiting
During 2021, 0.4 thousand counterfeit or forged MDL banknotes
were registered in the banking system, about 46.0% less than in
2020. Compared to the number of genuine banknotes in circulation
at the end of the reference period, which amounted to 317.9
million banknotes, the number of counterfeit banknotes remains
at an exceptionally low level and represents about one counterfeit
banknote per 1 million genuine banknotes in circulation.
The highest number of counterfeits was recorded for the MDL 100
nominal banknote, with 207 counterfeits, followed by the MDL 20
nominal banknote with 143 counterfeits, the MDL 50 banknote
with 19 counterfeits, the MDL 200 banknote with 14 counterfeits
and the MDL 1,000 banknote with 10 counterfeits.
As a basis for comparison, in 2020, the number of counterfeits of
national currency totalled 0.7 thousand banknotes, and the
highest
number
of
counterfeits
was
recorded
for
MDL100
banknotes, totalling 569 banknotes, MDL 20 – 108 banknotes,
MDL 50 – 8 banknotes, MDL 200 – 27 banknotes and MDL 1,000 –
16 banknotes.
The share of counterfeit banknotes by nominal value identiﬁed
during 2021 and 2020 is shown in Chart 6.16 and Chart 6.19
respectively.
In order to combat counterfeiting of monetary signs, the National
Bank of Moldova further recommends the public to study the
security features of banknotes described on the NBM website
(access the link here https://www.bnm.md/ro/content/elementele-
de-siguranta-pe-leul-moldovenesc) and in making daily cash
payments to be vigilant against the phenomenon of counterfeiting,
verifying the authenticity of banknotes and coins, and in case of
suspicion, immediately inform the Ministry of Internal Affairs.
At the same time, the NBM cooperates regularly with the Ministry
of Internal Affairs in withdrawing counterfeit banknotes from
circulation,
discouraging
and
keeping
counterfeiting
to
a
minimum, and ensures that the banking community is informed in
an
operational
manner
about
methods
of
detecting
and
withdrawing counterfeits from circulation.

Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
6.3
Numismatics issuance
According to the Law No.
548/1995 on the National Bank of
Moldova, the National Bank of Moldova launched in 2021 a new
numismatic issue of 8 anniversary and commemorative coins
with various themes, including a set of 3 commemorative coins
dedicated to the 30th anniversary of the proclamation of the
independence of the Republic of Moldova, reﬂecting famous titles
of verses that have become slogans – "A freedom, holy freedom",
"My heart is Moldova", "While we live on this land". The mintages
were differentiated according to the theme and the metal from
which the coins were made, i.e., 2,550 silver coins and 150 gold
coins were issued.
Table A.17 includes details of the anniversary and commemorative
coins related to the 2021 issuance.


101
Chapter 7
Legal
and
regulatory
framework
development activity
7.1
Prudential regulation, regulation of
accounting evidence in banks
During 2021, in order to apply the provisions of Law on Banks’
Activity, the National Bank of Moldova made changes to the
following legal documents:
• to Regulation on banks’ own funds and capital requirements
No. 109/2018. The amendments to the Regulation aimed at
specifying some issues related to the inclusion of interim or
year-end proﬁts in the core tier 1 own funds. Thus, it has
been speciﬁed that for unaudited interim and year-end
proﬁts, the results of the veriﬁcation should be recorded in
an audit report on the ﬁnancial statements showing that the
given proﬁts have been appropriately reﬂected by the bank
in accordance with the principles set out in the accounting
framework.
At the same time, the provisions related to the NBM approval
in the context of distributions have been adjusted, i.e. the
need to obtain approval for distributions of proﬁts to
shareholders
and/or
interest
payments
to
holders
of
additional tier 1 own funds instruments has been provided
for,
if
the
NBM
considers
that
this
will
not
lead
to
non-compliance with own funds requirements or other
prudential indicators or jeopardise the stability of the bank.
• to Regulation on the framework for the administration of
banking activity No. 322/2018, approved by the Decision of
the Executive Board of the National Bank of Moldova.
These amendments were conditioned by the need to
establish a comprehensive regulatory framework for the
implementation
of
the
Internal
Liquidity
Adequacy
Assessment Process (ILAAP) in banks, the establishment of
provisions related to the methodology for calculating the
potential changes in the economic value of the bank due to
changes
in
interest
rates
(IRRBB),
as
well
as
the
establishment of the method of informing the National Bank
of Moldova on the convening of the general meeting of
shareholders.
• to Regulation on holdings in Bank equity No. 127/2013 and
Regulation on requirements regarding the members of the
governing body of the bank, the ﬁnancial holding company or
mixed holding, the heads of a branch of a bank from another
state, the persons holding key positions and the liquidator of
the bank in liquidation process No. 292/2018.


102
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
These amendments concern the procedure for obtaining the
NBM approvals for the acquisition of shares in a bank or for
the appointment of the governing body of the bank, the
heads of a branch of a bank from another state and persons
holding key positions. Thus, these approvals can be obtained
not only in paper form, but also in electronic form through
the WEB portal of the NBM Information System.
The submission of documents required for obtaining these
NBM approvals in electronic form is possible if the applicant
holds the public key certiﬁcate, according to the Law on
electronic signature and electronic document. In order to
obtain the right to submit the application, the documents
and
the
necessary
information,
the
applicant
or
the
authorized person shall register through the NBM WEB portal
as a user in accordance with the NBM Information System
WEB Portal User Guidelines.
The possibility to obtain in electronic form the NBM’s
approvals for the acquisition of shares of a bank, as well as
for the appointment by the bank or branch of a bank from
another state of members of the governing body of the bank,
the heads of a branch of a bank from another state and the
persons holding key positions will reduce costs, efforts, time
allocated and will make the process more efﬁcient for both
the applicant and the NBM.
Submitting the application in electronic form will make the
process more environmentally friendly, as well as ensure
enhanced health protection measures for applicants and
employees of the NBM in pandemic conditions.
• to Regulation on outsourcing the bank’s activities and
operations No. 46/2020 and Regulation on external audit of
banks No. 118/2018.
The amendments were conditioned by the streamlining of
the audit process of outsourced material activities related to
card payment processing (item 44 sub item 4) of Regulation
No.
46/2020 on outsourcing the bank’s activities and
operations, the adjustment of the NBM regulations to the rule
provided for in Article 50 paragraph (7) of the Law No.
271/2017 on the audit of ﬁnancial statements and Regulation
No. 118/2018 on external audit of banks to the rule provided
for in Article 88 paragraph (6) of the Law No. 1134/1997 on
joint stock companies.
Thus, the amendments made included:
- amendments related to the criteria for the approval of the
audit company in the framework of the annual audit of
payment by card processing activities in the context of item
44 subitem 4) of Regulation No. 46/2020;
- adjustment of the regulatory acts of the National Bank of
Moldova in the context of Art.
50 para.7) of the Law No.
271/2017 on the audit of ﬁnancial statements regarding the
exclusion of the qualiﬁcation certiﬁcate of the auditor of
ﬁnancial institutions (repeal of the Regulation on the
issuance of the qualiﬁcation certiﬁcate of the auditor of
ﬁnancial institutions and item 5 subitem 3) letter b) of the
Regulation No. 118/2018);
- adjustment of Regulation No. 118/2018 (amendment of item
23 of the Regulation) in the context of Article 88 paragraph


Chapter 7. Legal and regulatory framework development activity
103
(6) of the Law No. 1134/1997 on joint stock companies, which
relates to the limitation of auditing companies performing the
audit of the bank’s annual ﬁnancial statements to enter into
other contracts with the bank regarding the audit for other
purposes;
- additions
to Regulation No.
118/2018
regarding the
facilitation by the bank of the timely submission by the audit
company to the National Bank of Moldova of complete
documents and information following the annual ﬁnancial
audit of the bank.
Aligning the banking legislation of the Republic of Moldova with
international
standards
by
improving
the
quantitative
and
qualitative mechanisms of bank management will contribute to
promoting a secure and stable banking sector,
increasing
transparency, trust, and attractiveness of the domestic banking
sector for potential investors and creditors of banks as well as for
depositors and clients, to the development of new ﬁnancial
products and services.


104
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
Chapter 8
International relations
8.1
International collaboration
International Monetary Fund (IMF)44
The International Monetary Fund is one of the main strategic
partners of the Republic of Moldova, providing valuable and
continuous support to our country, promoting, and supporting the
reforms necessary to ensure ﬁnancial stability and sustainable
economic growth.
During 2021, in the context of the initiative to support its member
countries in their efforts to revive their economies and remove as
soon as possible the negative economic consequences of the crisis
generated by the COVID-19 pandemic, the IMF Board of Governors
approved the largest allocation of Special Drawing Rights (SDR)45
in the IMF history, of around USD 650 billion, a decision that took
effect on August 23, 2021. Thus, the Republic of Moldova was
allocated SDR 165.3 million (the equivalent of USD 233.346 million),
these funds being transferred by the NBM to the accounts of the
Ministry of Finance, in accordance with the provisions of Law No.
138 of October 14, 2021 on the use of Special Drawing Rights
allocated by the International Monetary Fund to the Republic of
Moldova.
Between September 27 and October 15, 2021, considering the
consultations provided for in Article IV of the IMF Charter47 and
the request of the authorities of the Republic of Moldova to agree
with the IMF on a new economic and ﬁnancial policy programme,
discussions took place between the IMF team of experts and the
authorities of the Republic of Moldova, including the NBM. At the
end of the consultations, the IMF experts and the authorities of
the Republic of Moldova reached an agreement on an economic
reform programme, which was subsequently approved by the IMF
44http://bnm.md/en/content/relatia-republicii-moldova-cu-fondul-monetar-
international-fmi
45Special Drawing Right (SDR) is an international reserve asset created by IMF.
The SDR value is determined on the basis of a currency basket consisting of 5
currencies: the US dollar, the euro, the pound sterling, the Chinese Renminbi yuan,
and the Japanese yen.
https://www.imf.org/en/About/Factsheets/Sheets/2016/08/01/14/51/Special-
Drawing-Right-SDR
46Volume recalculated in USD at the exchange rate on the date of transfer of
funds by the NBM to the accounts of the Ministry of Finance.
47The IMF engages annually (as a rule) in bilateral discussions with member
states, in accordance with the provisions of Article IV of the IMF Charter. An IMF
mission visits the member state, collects economic and ﬁnancial information, and
discusses with the political authorities about the economic developments in that
country. Following the visit, the mission prepares a report that forms the basis of
discussions within the IMF Executive Board.
https://www.imf.org/external/pubs/ft/aa/pdf/aa.pdf


Chapter 8. International relations
105
Executive Board48. The value of the ﬁnancial assistance provided
by the IMF to support the implementation of this programme is
SDR 400 million (which is 232% of Moldova’s IMF quota),
equivalent to about USD 558.3 million, and the duration of the
programme is 40 months. The programme is ﬁnanced under the
Extended Credit Facility (ECF)49 and the Extended Fund Facility
(EFF)50. Immediately after the approval of the IMF Executive
Board’s decision to ﬁnancially support the implementation of the
new reform programme, the ﬁrst tranche of budget support credit
was disbursed through ECF and EFF in the amount of USD 79.8
million.
The objectives of the new reform programme mentioned above
include
further
strengthening
the
NBM
independence,
the
ﬁnancial sector supervisory framework,
the ﬁnancial crisis
management framework, the macroprudential policy framework
and reforming and strengthening the non-bank ﬁnancial sector in
line with European and international best practices, by assigning
from 2023 the NBM new tasks of licensing/authorisation/issuance
of state registration, regulation and supervision of the activity of
some entities in the non-bank ﬁnancial sector.
Along with the adoption of the decision to support the new
programme, the IMF Executive Board also approved the report on
the consultations with the Republic of Moldova under Article IV of
the IMF Charter for 2021, which referred to the progress made by
the NBM in rehabilitating the banking sector and strengthening
macro-ﬁnancial
stability
as
a
result
of
the
successful
implementation in 2016-2019 of the reforms supported by the IMF
under the previous economic and ﬁnancial policy programme.
In 2021, total disbursements related to servicing of IMF loans to
the NBM amounted to SDR 32.7 million (equivalent to USD 46.6
million), of which disbursements for the principal amount of the
loan amounted to SDR 31.96 million (equivalent to USD 45.58
million) and disbursements for interest on loans amounted to SDR
0.7 million (equivalent to USD 1.0 million).
As of December 31, 2021, the balance of the NBM’s ﬁnancial
commitments to the IMF constituted SDR 66.01 million (USD 92.39
million) of which: EFF related commitments: SDR 55.13 million
(USD 77.16 million); ECF related commitments: SDR 10.88 million
(USD 15.23 million).
Given that the Republic of Moldova is represented in the IMF
Board of Governors by the Governor of the NBM and the First
48IMF Executive Board is one of the bodies of the IMF, consisting of 24 Executive
Directors (elected by member countries) and the General Director of IMF, chairing
the meetings of the Board. The Executive Board is responsible for the day-to-day
running of the IMF activity and usually meets several times each week and conducts
its work largely on the basis of documents prepared by IMF management and staff.
https://www.imf.org/external/np/sec/memdir/eds.aspx
49The Extended Credit Facility (ECF) is an IMF ﬁnancing tool through which
eligible (low-income) countries can beneﬁt from concessional ﬁnancial assistance
in a more ﬂexible form and better tailored to their various needs, including during
crises (e.g., in the case of protracted balance of payments problems).
https://www.imf.org/en/About/Factsheets/Sheets/2016/08/02/21/04/Extended-
Credit-Facility
50The Extended Fund Facility (EFF) is an IMF ﬁnancing tool for countries with
large balance of payments imbalances caused by structural impediments or slow
economic growth and certain inherently precarious balance of payments positions.
https://www.imf.org/en/About/Factsheets/Sheets/2016/08/01/20/56/Extended-
Fund-Facility


106
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
Deputy Governor of the NBM, who hold the positions of Governor
and Alternate Governor, respectively, the NBM representatives
attended the spring and annual meetings held online between
April 5-11 and October 11-17, 2021. During these meetings, topics
were discussed on the transition to a green, digital, and sustainable
recovery, as well as the macro-policy response to pandemics,
ﬁnancing needs, including for low-income countries, policies to
maintain debt sustainability and improve transparency, policies to
combat climate risks and SDR allocation.
Also, on March 25, 2021, the Governor of the NBM attended the
high-level teleconference "The rule of Law in the EU: the current
situation and its implications for candidate and neighbouring
countries", organized by the National Bank of Belgium as a
platform for discussion, exchange of experience and views for
governors of the IMF constituency to which the Republic of
Moldova belongs51.
European Union (EU)52
In 2021, in the context of the implementation of the common
objectives set by the Republic of Moldova and EU in the framework
of the Association Agreement, the NBM continued to strengthen
cooperative relations with EU central banks that are responsible
for the supervision of EU credit institutions that have branches in
the Republic of Moldova.
Thus, on June 11, 2021, the Cooperation Agreement between the
National Bank of Moldova and the National Bank of Romania was
signed, which aims to develop and expand cooperation in a wide
range of areas, including licensing and supervision of banks;
licensing and supervision of payment services providers and
electronic
money
issuers;
ﬁnancial
market
infrastructures;
prevention
and
combating
money
laundering
and
terrorist
ﬁnancing; cash operations; crisis management, etc.
In the context of the European integration process, during 2021
the NBM examined and submitted proposals to the Moldovan
authorities regarding the draft of the Republic of Moldova –
European Union Association Agenda for the period 2021-2027,
which
was
prepared
with
the
aim
of
prioritising
the
implementation of the commitments undertaken under the
Association Agreement and includes short and long-term reform
actions in the areas of justice, combating corruption, ensuring the
rule
of
law,
economic
cooperation,
digital
transformation,
strengthening central bank independence, strengthening ﬁnancial
sector regulation, etc.
51Within the IMF, the Republic of Moldova is part of the group of countries
(constituency) that includes: Andorra, Armenia, Belgium, Bosnia and Herzegovina,
Bulgaria, Cyprus, Croatia, Georgia, Israel, Luxembourg, Republic of North
Macedonia, Montenegro, Kingdom of the Netherlands, Romania, Republic of
Moldova and Ukraine.
The IMF Executive Director representing the countries
in this group, as of November 2020, is Paul Hilbers, elected for a 4-year term.
52The collaboration relations between the Republic of Moldova and the European
Union (EU) is developed on the basis of the Association Agreement between the
Republic of Moldova, on the one hand, and the European Union and the European
Atomic Energy Community and their Member States, on the other hand, signed in
Brussels on 27 June 2014, with effect from 1 July 2016 (provisionally applied from 1
September 2014).

of EUR 60 million (equivalent to USD 67.9 million) under the

European
Bank
for
Reconstruction
and
Development
(EBRD)55
The EBRD is one of the most important development partners for
the Republic of Moldova, making a valuable contribution both to
improving the business environment by supporting the private
sector through ﬁnancing and advisory projects, and to developing
policy dialogue aimed at increasing transparency in the banking
sector.
In the context of the implementation of the Country Strategy of
the EBRD for the Republic of Moldova for 2017-2022, the NBM is
constantly cooperating with the EBRD to restructure and increase
the resilience of the domestic banking sector.
The EBRD’s presence as a shareholder in the banking sector
of the Republic of Moldova contributes to improving corporate
governance, which complements the NBM’s efforts to admit only
quality shareholders to the sector, who fully meet the criteria of
good repute and professionalism.
During 2021, the NBM Governor, holding the position of Alternate
Governor in the EBRD Board of Governors, attended the 30th
Annual Meeting of the EBRD and the EBRD Governors’ Roundtable,
which were held online on July 1, 2021, where the following topics
were discussed: EBRD’s business review for 2020; EBRD’s climate
ambitions; the Strategy Implementation Plan 2021-2023 of the
Strategic and Capital Framework 2021-2025 approved in 2020;
building better economies; possible EBRD expansion into sub-
Saharan Africa and Iraq, etc. Moreover, the NBM presented the
position of the Republic of Moldova on the topics discussed during
these meetings.

European
Automated
