House
Association
(EACHA)56
In 2021,
the NBM continued cooperation with EACHA,
by
exchanging information and experience in the ﬁeld of payment
processing houses.
On May 25-27, 2021, the NBM representatives attended the annual
forum "EACHA payments forum 2021" and the EACHA Plenary
Meeting. The participation of the NBM in these events takes place
in the context of the achievement of the NBM’s strategic objectives,
such as: the accession of the banking sector to the single euro
payment area (Single European Payments Area – SEPA) and the
implementation of instant payments57.
International Operational Risk Working Group (IORWG)58
In 2021, the NBM continued to participate in the activities carried
out by the IORWG, by providing responses to the requests and
studies initiated by this working group, the most important being:
• "Survey Maturity Model Trends Best Practices 2021" –
information on the maturity model of operational risk
management in the NBM and practices in this ﬁeld;
• participation in the 16th Annual Conference, organised by
the Central Bank of Ireland, one of the main topics being
resilience in the COVID-19 period;
• "Survey on Benchmarking" – study on regulatory framework,
processes, and risk management practices in the areas of
enterprise risk management; business continuity; information
security and compliance;
• survey, initiated by the NBM, on risk management practices
of non-compliant execution of the purchase contract and the
limitation of the supplier’s liability.
56The European Automated Clearing House Association (EACHA) is a non-proﬁt
organization of clearing system administrators and payment processing centers.
Several working groups in the payments sector are organized within EACHA to
address issues such as interoperability within the Single Euro Payments Area
(SEPA). EACHA currently consists of 26 members from 23 countries. The NBM,
as administrator of the Automated Interbank Payment System (AIPS), became a
member of EACHA in 2014.
57Instant payments are electronic payments of small value which are processed
in real time, 24 hours a day, 365 days a year, where the funds are made available
to the recipient/beneﬁciary immediately for use.
58The International Operational Risk Working Group (IORWG) is a working group
for central banks and monetary and supervisory authorities, which aims to
exchange experience, innovate the methodological framework and share best
practices between central banks in the ﬁeld of operational risk management. The
NBM became a member of IORWG in 2015.

59Centre of Excellence in Finance (CEF) is an international organisation with
the mission of supporting the implementation of reforms in the ﬁelds of public
ﬁnance and ﬁnancial-banking in South-Eastern Europe through training activities.
CEF was established in 2001 under the Stability Pact for South-Eastern Europe
by the Slovak government, at the initiative of the Slovenian Ministry of Finance
and in close cooperation with other Ministries of Finance of the former Yugoslav
countries and Albania. In 2015, the CEF became an international organisation after
14 successful years as a regional institution. The Republic of Moldova became a
member of CEF by approving Law No. 115 on the ratiﬁcation of the Agreement
on the Establishment of the CEF on May 28, 2015. The Republic of Moldova is
represented in the Board of Directors of the CEF by the NBM and the Ministry of
Finance, each holding a voting right in the decision-making process.
60Group of Banking Supervisors from Central and Eastern Europe (BSCEE) is
a regional organisation, without legal personality, which promotes cooperation
between banking supervisors of Central and Eastern European countries. The
National Bank of Moldova became a member of BSCEE in 1996, following the
signing of the Agreement on the Rules of Organisation and Management of the
BSCEE Group.

61Euromoney Institutional Investor PLC ("Euromoney") is a global multi-branded
information company that provides critical data, price reports, information, analysis
and organizes events attended by representatives from the ﬁnancial services,

62JVI is a regional training centre, primarily for ofﬁcials in the public sector
from countries of Central, Eastern and South-Eastern Europe, the Caucasus, and
Central Asia. It was established in 1992 by the Austrian authorities (represented by
the Federal Ministry of Finance and the Austrian Central Bank), the International
Monetary Fund and several other international organisations.

sector stability, macroeconomic and ﬁnancial statistics, and many other specialised
topics.

During 2021, the NBM continued its technical cooperation with its

in

In this context, in order to strengthen the function of ﬁnancial

114
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
our country, with the support of the International Monetary
Fund (IMF), which continues to be one of the Republic of Moldova
main development partners, the Stability Analysis of the ﬁnancial
sector of the Republic of Moldova has been performed (Financial
Sector Stability Review, FSSR).
This large-scale exercise involved, in addition of the NBM, of other
key institutions whose activities have an impact on the ﬁnancial
sector, such as the National Commission for Financial Markets, the
Ministry of Finance, the Deposit Guarantee Fund in the Banking
System, the Central Securities Depository, the Ministry of Economy,
and the Ministry of Education, Culture and Research and some
commercial banks.
Thus, the mission assessed ﬁve areas as
follows:
• ﬁnancial sector supervision;
• ﬁnancial safety mechanism;
• ﬁnancial stability, macroprudential policy and stress testing;
• ﬁnancial market infrastructure;
• ﬁnancial inclusion and integrity.
Following
the
assessment
exercise,
the
IMF
experts
have
developed a roadmap regarding the technical assistance needs to
improve the activities that underpin the ﬁnancial stability function.
Similarly, with the support of the IMF, the practical activities
regarding the compilation of statistics related to ﬁnancial accounts,
sectoral balance sheets (FASBS) and balance sheet statistics at
the level of annual stocks for 2015-2020 were continued and
the information to be used for the compilation of the FASBS was
reviewed and updated. At the same time, for the non-ﬁnancial
companies, indicators have been developed and evaluated to
estimate the compilation of statistics related to ﬁnancial and
quarterly sectoral balance sheets accounts.
The European Union (EU) is among the NBM’s key development
partners. During 2021, the NBM continued to beneﬁt from the
assistance of the EU High-Level Adviser for the ﬁnancial services
sector in the context of the Mission of the EU Senior Advisers
2019-2021 for the Republic of Moldova63, project extended until
63The project is funded by the European Union with an implementation period
of three years, from January 2019 to December 2021. Its main objective is to
provide support to the Government of the Republic of Moldova in implementing its
reform agenda, in this case, assistance in strengthening the capacity needed to
implement the Moldova - EU Association Agreement, including the implementation
of the provisions of the Deep and Comprehensive Free Trade Area, as well as to
ensure the following post-liberalisation actions of the visa regime. In particular, the
project aims at:
- strengthening policymaking at sectoral level, including the strategic planning
and policy management capacities of public authorities operating in the
sectors selected for assistance;
- strengthening the knowledge and awareness of stakeholders on EU policies,
legislation, and regulations;
- providing guidance in order to ensure the political, institutional, and ﬁnancial
sustainability of the reforms planned in these sectors for their efﬁcient
implementation.
To ﬁnd out more about the EU Mission of High-Level Advisors to the Republic of
Moldova, please visit the website: http://www.eu-advisers.md.

115
2022. This high-level expert provides advice to the NBM in the
areas of banking supervision and the implementation of risk-based
supervision, effective communication, ﬁnancial stability, payment

as to facilitate cooperation with various local and international

At the same time, thanks to the support provided by the EU
through the instrument of administrative cooperation Twinning64,
on October 18,
2021,
the Twinning project "Strengthening
supervision, corporate governance and risk management in the
ﬁnancial sector" started. The project implementation period is 2
years, and its beneﬁciaries are the NBM and the National
Commission for Financial Market (NCFM).
Thus, by October 2023, the NBM and the NCFM will beneﬁt from the
valuable experience and expertise of EU partner institutions. The
National Bank of Romania, as senior partner, and the Central Bank
of the Netherlands and the Central Bank of Lithuania, as junior
partners, in collaboration with the Romanian Financial Supervisory
Authority and the Romanian National Authority for Consumer
Protection, have come together in a consortium to help the NBM
and the NCFM further develop their capabilities, following EU best
practices to ensure a high level of ﬁnancial sector stability for the
beneﬁt of citizens and businesses.
The overall objective of the Twinning project is to support the
Republic of Moldova in implementing reforms related to the
Moldova-EU
Association
Agreement
and
the
Moldova-EU
Association Agenda and to strengthen supervision, corporate
governance, and risk management in the ﬁnancial sector.
The Twinning project consists of ﬁve major components with
corresponding outputs and activities as follows:
Component 1: Strengthening the macroprudential framework to
address the build-up of systemic risks;
Component 2:
Align the legal framework for insurance and
motor liability insurance supervision with the EU acquis;
Component 3: Develop and implement a new legal framework
to strengthen the supervision of non-bank credit companies;
Component 4: Review, develop and improve the NBM’s internal
regulations
on
monitoring
activities
of
ﬁnancial
market
infrastructure and payment services;
Component 5: Align the legislation and regulatory framework in
the Republic of Moldova with the requirements of the Single Euro
Payments Area (SEPA) scheme and prepare for SEPA accession.
Also, thanks to the support provided by the EU, the NBM and 15
other institutions in the Republic of Moldova with responsibilities
in the ﬁeld of preventing and combating money laundering and
terrorist
ﬁnancing,
beneﬁted
in
2018-2021
from
technical
assistance within another Twinning project "Efﬁciency of the
system for preventing and combating money laundering and
terrorist ﬁnancing in the Republic of Moldova", the implementation

Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
period being extended until June 2022. The main objective of this
project is to contribute to the strengthening and development of
the supervisory mechanism of reporting entities, taking into
account the provisions of the Law No. 308/2017 on preventing
and
combating
money
laundering
and
terrorist
ﬁnancing,
including by implementing robust internal procedures, assessing
the risks to which entities are exposed, continuing education and

Another important donor of technical assistance to the NBM is
the World Bank. In 2021, representatives of the World Bank’s
Financial Sector Advisory Centre (FinSAC) continued to provide
support to the NBM in the context of strengthening the bank
resolution framework. FinSac experts provided advice in order to
operationalize the provisions of the Law No. 232/2016 on banks
recovery and resolution, related to the domestic recapitalization
tool, as well as improving resolution planning activities.
Also, with the support of the World Bank, the NBM representatives
continued to beneﬁt from international training and certiﬁcation
opportunities in the professional ﬁelds within the program of
the Investment Advisory and Management Agreement (Reserves
Advisory & Management Partnership65 (RAMP)), which contributes
to the continuous development of NBM employees and to the
application of the latest standards in the respective ﬁelds of
activity.
Similarly, under the RAMP programme, NBM representatives have
also beneﬁted from technical support to strengthen their credit
risk assessment capacity.
In the context of the need to reform and strengthen the non-
bank ﬁnancial sector in line with European and international good
practices, IMF, and World Bank experts, in partnership with the EU
High-level Adviser for Financial Services sector, have contributed
to the development of a smooth and effective transition plan
for the NBM to take over new tasks speciﬁc to the non-bank
ﬁnancial sector.
This reform was imposed by the approval by
the Parliament of the Republic of Moldova of certain amendments
to the legislation in force, which establish that the NBM will take
over from the NCFM, as of July 1, 2023, the powers of licensing,
authorization, as well as issuing the opinion for state registration,
regulation and supervision of the activity of insurers, reinsurers and
insurance and/or reinsurance intermediaries, the National Bureau
of Motor Vehicles Insurers of the Republic of Moldova, savings and
loan associations, credit history bureaus and non-banking credit
organisations.
Respectively, during 2021 a set of principles for future cooperation
between the NBM and the NCFM and a draft Joint Action Plan
regarding the takeover of the above-mentioned powers from the
NCFM were developed and presented for consultation.
Ensuring the stability of the banking system by strengthening the
function and framework of preventing and combating money
laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing is one of the priorities of the
NBM. Thus, within the framework of the technical assistance
project from the United States Agency for International

Also, in the framework of the project "Moldova Financial Sector

In

NBM, along with other institutions, is actively involved in actions

Another partner in the ﬁeld of promoting ﬁnancial education
during 2021 was the Good Governance Fund (GGF), funded by the
Government of the United Kingdom of Great Britain and Northern
Ireland. Thanks to the support provided by the GGF, the NBM has
made a signiﬁcant contribution to increasing the level of ﬁnancial
education and ﬁnancial inclusion aiming to improve the ﬁnancial
wellbeing of the population. In this regard, thanks to the technical
assistance that the NBM beneﬁtted from during the reporting
period, the access of the beneﬁciaries of education and ﬁnancial
inclusion to innovative digital products was increased, the external
communication process of the NBM was improved through digital
communication and by adapting existing informational content for
various target groups. At the same time, the capacity to create
multimedia products to promote the information content to be
published on the NBM’s communication channels for the purpose
of ﬁnancial education of the general public was enhanced.


118
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
An equally important contribution was made by the GGF’s support
to the area of human resources management within the NBM.
In this context, advice was provided for the establishment of
the internal training centre, which entails the creation of internal
management training capacity, development of managerial and
leadership skills for mid-level NBM managers.
In the same line, in order to strengthen institutional capacity and
efﬁciency, in particular to continue the series of efforts to
implement a new integrated human resources management
system and to optimize the use of professional development
resources, the Ministry of Finance of Slovakia through the Centre
of Excellence in Finance (CEF) headquartered in Ljubljana
offered the NBM the opportunity to get acquainted with its
experience in the ﬁeld of human resources management and
development. NBM experts improved their knowledge on human
resources management,
development and recruitment and
ﬁnalised the action plan for strengthening the institutional
capacity of the NBM in cooperation with the CEF.
At the same time, NBM representatives attended several trainings
organised online by the CEF, both in professional areas related to
the core business of the NBM and in the areas of governance and
support activities. At the same time, at the request of the CEF, the
NBM staff participated as trainers in several trainings organised
by the CEF.
In 2021, the NBM further strengthened its bilateral relations with
other central banks, in terms of technical assistance, with the aim
of assimilating the best practices in the managed ﬁelds, as well
as sharing its institutional experience with colleagues from other
countries, with similar attributions.
It should be noted the constant technical cooperation between the
NBM and the Central Bank of Germany. During 2021, in order
to develop the framework related to emergency credit facilities,
the NBM beneﬁted from advice from German experts with the
aim of determining the potential areas for improvement in this
ﬁeld. In the same way, the NBM representatives participated in a
series of online training sessions in the ﬁeld of monetary issuance,
organized by the Central Bank of Germany, for the employees
of several European central banks, addressing aspects related to
good cash management, effective processing, IT equipment and
applications, methods, operational risks, etc.
Another permanent partner remains to be the National Bank
of Belgium.
In order to determine the degree of compliance
of the internal audit activity within the NBM with international
standards and the Code of Ethics for Internal Auditors, in 2021,
with the support of the experts of the National Bank of Belgium, the
respective assessment of the professional practice in the ﬁeld of
internal audit was carried out and the rating "General compliance"
was obtained, which represents the highest degree of compliance.
In addition to the valuable technical support that the NBM has
received, the NBM continues to provide technical assistance to
other central banks through the exchange of experience and best
practices in banking-related areas.
In this regard, NBM experts attended the online seminar organized
by the Central Bank of France on central bank transformation,

120
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
Chapter 9
Institutional
and
organizational
framework
9.1
Corporate
Governance
of
the
National Bank
The Law on the National Bank of Moldova provides the legal
authority and the governance framework of the National Bank.
The structure of corporate governance includes governing bodies,
committees that support governing bodies, and external and
internal control levels. This structure is complemented by the
compliance and ethics framework, which includes the Code of
Conduct for NBM Employees, the Anti-Fraud Policy of the National
Bank of Moldova, the Information Security Policy, as well as the
public access to normative acts, statistics, and publications of the
NBM.
Governing bodies of the National Bank
Governor
The Governor of the National Bank is appointed by the Parliament
at the proposal of the President of the Parliament.
In accordance with the Law on the National Bank of Moldova, the
Governor is responsible for formulating monetary and foreign
exchange policy initiatives for submission to the Executive Board,
as well as their execution.
Also, the Governor organizes and
manages the activity of the bank and represents it in relations
with any legal or natural person both in the Republic of Moldova
and abroad, issues orders and mandatory provisions for the NBM’s
employees, controls their execution.
Supervisory Board
The Supervisory Board consists of seven members appointed by
the Parliament for a seven-year term: including 4 non-salaried
members of the NBM, and 3 members belonging to the executive
structure of the National Bank: the Governor, the First Deputy
Governor,
and a Deputy Governor.
The Chairman of the
Supervisory Board is the Governor of the NBM. From January to
November 2021, the Supervisory Board operated without a
member of the executive structure of the NBM. On December 3,
2021, the Supervisory Board appointed the third member of the
executive structure of the NBM.


Chapter 9. Institutional and organizational framework
121
In exercising its powers attributed by law, the Supervisory Board
(i) approves the annual report and the annual ﬁnancial statements;
(ii) adopts the internal control system standards, continuously
checks and assesses the operation of the internal control system;
(iii) appoints the controller general upon the proposal of the Audit
Committee and establishes the terms of his/her remuneration,
(iv) adopts the rules of professional ethics; (v) determines the
remuneration fund of the National Bank, the level of remuneration
of the members of the Executive Board and the amount of monthly
indemnities of the members of the Supervisory Board who are not
members of the Executive Board; (vi) submits proposals to increase
the National Bank’s capital; (vii) approves the National Bank’s
estimates of expenses and investment allowances and monitors
their execution; (viii) selects, on a tender basis, the external audit
organization; (ix) establishes the method in which the National
Bank Committees are created and operated; (x) determines the
nominal value, the design of banknotes and metallic coins, the way
of putting into circulation and the conditions for their withdrawal
from circulation; (xi) approves the internal regulations relating to
the exercise of its powers. The Supervisory Board submits to the
Parliament, at least annually, reports on the developed supervision
activity.
For the performance of its duties during 2021, 15 meetings of
the Supervisory Board were convened, and 47 decisions were
approved, with the following issues:
• Selection of the external audit organization of the ﬁnancial
statements, accounts, and records of the National Bank of
Moldova for 2020-2024;
• Approval of the graphic designs, denominations and putting
into circulation of the jubilee and commemorative coins and
the commemorative metal circulation coin 2021 issue;
• Approval of the Report on internal audit activity in 2020;
• Approval of the expenditure estimate and investment
allowances of the National Bank of Moldova and reports on
the execution of the expenditure estimate and investment
allowances of the National Bank of Moldova;
• Approval of the reports on the level of implementation in
2020 of the projects of the National Bank of Moldova;
• Approval of the Report on the management of operational
risk in 2020 in the National Bank of Moldova;
• Examination of the direct or indirect ﬁnancial interests of
the members of the governing bodies and members of their
families;
• Approval of the Report of the Audit Committee of the National
Bank of Moldova on the monitoring activity in 2020;
• Approval of the Report of the National Bank of Moldova for
2020;
• Approval of the Report of the Supervisory Board of the
National Bank of Moldova on supervisory activity in 2020;
• Appointment of the Controller General of the National Bank
of Moldova – Director of the Internal Audit Department;


122
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
• Approval of the Financial Statements of the National Bank of
Moldova for the year ended on December 31, 2020;
• Approval of the Consolidated Annual Report for the year
ended on December 31, 2020;
• Approval of the Annual Internal Audit Plan 2022;
• Approval of the indicative work programme of the Supervisory
Board for 2022.
During 2021, the Supervisory Board issued for publication 4
decisions in the Ofﬁcial Monitor of the Republic of Moldova
(according to Annex A.18).
In its activity, the Supervisory Board has continuously cooperated
with the representatives of the development partners of the
Republic of Moldova and other ﬁnancial-banking institutions on
subjects of common interest. The Supervisory Board promoted
transparency in decision-making and monitored the activities of
the NBM with reference to its legal powers.
Executive Board
The Executive Board is composed of 5 members appointed by the
Parliament: the Governor, the First Deputy Governor and three
Deputy Governors. The Chairman of the Executive Board is the
Governor of the National Bank.
During the reference year, 2 deputy governors served as members
of the EB with expired mandates until the appointment of new
members, according to Law No. 548/1995 on the National Bank
of Moldova, Article 23 paragraph (8). In November and December
2021, the Parliament adopted decisions on the appointment of
3 new members of the Executive Board (one position of Deputy
Governor of the EB was vacant): Mr Constantin Schendra, Ms
Tatiana Ivanicichina and Mr Arcadie Albul. The term of ofﬁce of
Deputy Governor Arcadie Albul will start on March 1, 2022.
According to Law No. 548/1995, the main duties of the Executive
Board are:
(i) to establish the monetary policy in the state,
including the level of interest rates on monetary policy tools, the
conditions for granting loans, the type and level of reserves that
banks are obliged to maintain with the National Bank; (ii) to
establish the foreign exchange policy in the state and the
exchange rate regime of the national currency; (iii) to decide on
the procedure of issuing of licenses, authorizations, permits,
approvals to be issued by the National Bank; (iv) to examine the
results of controls performed on the entities supervised by the
National Bank and adopt the related decisions; (v) to decide on
the issuance of the National Bank’s claims, their volume and
conditions of issuance; (vi) to adopt the normative acts of the
National Bank; (vii) to ensure the implementation of the decisions
of the Supervisory Board; (viii) to plan and organize the current
activity of the National Bank.
Its Chair shall convene meetings of the Executive Board at least
once a month.
During 2021, in order to perform its duties, the Executive Board
convened 98 meetings,
during which 271 resolutions were
examined and approved.
These resolutions concerned the following areas:


Chapter 9. Institutional and organizational framework
123
• Monetary and foreign exchange policy of the National Bank
of Moldova
• Payment system oversight policy in the Republic of Moldova
• Accounting policy of the National Bank of Moldova
• Banking regulation and supervision
• Accounting records
• Balance of payments
• Approval and amendment of internal acts of the National
Bank of Moldova
During 2021, the Executive Board issued for publications 29
decisions in the Ofﬁcial Monitor of The Republic of Moldova
(according to Annex A.18 and A.19).
The
Executive
Board
has
continuously
promoted
the
implementation of monetary policy tools and measures to ensure
and maintain price stability; ensure the economy with the national
currency and promote the credibility of the national currency;
regulate and supervise banks and maintain ﬁnancial stability;
regulate
and
supervise
the
activity
of
ﬁnancial
market
infrastructures, as well as the efﬁciency, safety, accessibility, and
innovativeness of payments.

Committees
In order to ensure the efﬁciency of the decision-making process,
the Audit Committee, the Investment Committee, and the Risk
Committee operate within the NBM. These standing committees


124
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
have their own rules of operation detailing their purpose,
composition, duties, and speciﬁc responsibilities.
Audit Committee
The
Audit
Committee
consists
of
three
members
of
the
Supervisory Board who are not employees of the National Bank.
The Audit Committee assists the Supervisory Board by advising
on: (i) monitoring the ﬁnancial reporting process of the NBM; (ii)
monitoring the effectiveness of the internal control and risk
management system; (iii) monitoring and directing the internal
audit function; (iv) monitoring the independence and work of the
external audit.
In order to ensure the work and fulﬁlment of its duties, in 2021,
the Audit Committee undertook a series of practical measures and
actions, the most important of which are:
• Examination of the results of the assessments presented by
the working group regarding the selection of the external
audit organization and the presentation to the Supervisory
Board of the selection proposal for the external audit of the
annual ﬁnancial statements, accounts, and registers of the
NBM for 2020-2024;
• Elaboration of the procedure for selection, examination, and
approval of the candidacy of the Comptroller General of the
NBM,
the
proposal
of
the
Supervisory
Board
for
the
appointment for the next 5 years and the approval of the job
description of the Comptroller General of the NBM - Director
of the Internal Audit Department;
• Examination of the activity reports and coordination of the
activity plans of the NBM Internal Audit Department;
• Evaluation of the results of the activity of the Controller
General for 2021;
• Reviewing with the representatives of the external audit
of the Financial Statements and their compliance with the
International Financial Reporting Standards;
• Examination of procedures for monitoring and controlling risk
exposures and compliance issues that could have a signiﬁcant
impact on the NBM Financial Statements;
• Formulation of proposals for improvement.
During the reporting period, the Audit Committee developed and
presented to the SB of the NBM recommendations for streamlining
the work of the Internal Audit Department,
improving and
developing the NBM’s business processes, including on the NBM’s
ﬁnancial reporting process; the process of consolidating the new
regulatory and methodological framework for managing the
NBM’s operational risks and creating a centralised system for
managing all risks; implementation of the recommendations put
forward
by
the
external
evaluator
in
the
External
Quality
Assessment Report of the Internal Audit Function related to the
development
of
professional
development
plans
for
IAD
employees, development of the strategic approach in audit
missions, development of an internal audit strategy.


Chapter 9. Institutional and organizational framework
125
Investment Committee
The Investment Committee (IC) is established to assist the
Executive Board of the NBM in reviewing investment policies and
strategies related to the management of the foreign exchange
reserves of the state. The Investment Committee is composed of
10 members, employees of the NBM, including 2 members of the
Executive Board, one of whom is the Chairman of the Committee.
IC meetings are convened, as a rule, on a monthly basis, and
extraordinary meetings are held in the case of signiﬁcant issues.
The Investment Committee held 12 ordinary meetings in 2021.
Each meeting discussed the performance of the investment
portfolio for the previous reporting month, developments and
forecasts related to the domestic foreign exchange market and
international ﬁnancial markets, developments in the ratings of
authorised counterparties and risks related to the investment
portfolio.
At the same time, they regularly discussed issues
regarding the short- and medium-term investment strategy of the
Strategic Asset Allocation (SAA).
An extraordinary IC meeting was also convened in December 2021
to review the SAA framework. In addition, tactical investment
decisions were considered with a view to optimising cost/return
indicators in the context of the low-rate environment, subject to
ensuring the safety and liquidity of investments.
Risk Committee
The Risk Committee, as part of the second line of defence, is
established to assist the Executive Board in order to ensure
objective direct oversight of the management of the operational
risk management system in the National Bank.
The Risk
Committee consists of 8 voting members, including one member
of the Executive Board - the Risk Committee Chairperson.
The Risk Committee meets quarterly in regular meetings.
If
necessary, or at the request of the Governor or any other member
of the Executive Board, it may convene ad hoc.
In 2021, the Risk Committee met in 4 regular meetings and 1 ad
hoc meeting.
The basic decisions taken concerned:
1. endorsement of the annual report on operational risk
management in 2020 in the National Bank of Moldova, of
dashboard and risk proﬁle for 2020;
2. acceptance of the assessed maturity level of the operational
risk management system in the NBM by December 31, 2020,
and its development objectives for 2021;
3. taking note of the quarterly reports on the results of the self-
assessment of operational risks on NBM business processes;
4. taking note of the NBM’s Operational Risk Proﬁle for the
processes assessed in 2021;
5. approve the proposed treatment measures to respond to the
risks in the yellow area of the risk proﬁle;


126
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
6. taking note of the information related to incidents with high
and major impact on business processes and the validation
of their impact level;
7. taking note of the measures applied to manage the risk of
business continuity generated by the external factor - the
outbreak caused by the COVID-19 virus;
8. taking note of the information on the work as member of the
International Operational Risk Working Group - IORWG.
Levels of control
External control
External audit
The external audit of the National Bank is performed annually for
the ﬁnancial statements, accounts and registers of the NBM, in
accordance with the international auditing standards, by an
independent auditing company of recognized reputation and
experience in the ﬁeld of auditing central banks and international
ﬁnancial institutions, selected by the Supervisory Board on the
basis of an auction. The report of the external auditor shall be
published together with the annual ﬁnancial statements of the
National Bank. The same external audit organisation may not be
appointed consecutively for a period exceeding 5 years.
For 2020-2024, the Supervisory Board has appointed "KPMG
Moldova" SRL as the external auditor of the NBM’s ﬁnancial
statements. For 2020 and 2021 unqualiﬁed opinion reports have
been issued.
IMF assessment of NBM credibility and security
Periodically, the credibility and security of central banks is
assessed by IMF experts in order to receive assurance in the
implementation and operation by the NBM of control, accounting,
reporting, and audit systems related to the management of
resources that are adequate for the integrity of its operations. The
assessment covers ﬁve key areas relevant to central bank control
and governance under the acronym ELRIC:
• E – external audit mechanism;
• L – legal framework and independence of the central bank;
• R – ﬁnancial reporting;
• I – internal audit mechanism;
• C – system of internal controls.
Audit of the Court of Accounts of the Republic of Moldova
The external public audit regarding the legality and regularity of
expenditure estimates and investment allocations is performed
by the Court of Accounts, being limited, according to the Law No.
548/1995, to examine the operational efﬁciency of decisions taken


Chapter 9. Institutional and organizational framework
127
by the management of the National Bank, with the exception of
those related to the implementation of the monetary and foreign
exchange policy of the National Bank and the management of the
State’s foreign exchange reserves.
Risk management and internal control
The National Bank aligns the governance of operational risk
management
and
the
internal
control
system
with
the
international standards COSO66, ISO 3100067, to good practices in
the ﬁeld (including the International Operational Risk Working
Group (IORWG), of which the National Bank is a member).
In the context of operational risk management, with the approval of
the Risk Management Framework in the National Bank of Moldova,
the Supervisory Board has established the risk appetite and risk
tolerance in the National Bank.
Risk management as well as internal control within the NBM are
established according to the three lines of defence.
The ﬁrst line of defence manages the risks and is represented by
the NBM subdivisions, which are also the "owners" of the risks.
The heads of the subdivisions (line managers) are responsible
for identifying and managing the risks inherent in the business
processes and the IT systems they own, properly establishing the
control activities in the risk management process. In 2021, the
activities of self-assessment of operational risks on the activity
processes continued with the establishment of control measures
to maintain the risks in the tolerance zone, with the reporting
of the results to the operational risk management function. The
subdivisions apply a set of control procedures on a daily basis to
the business processes and systems they manage, ensuring their
effectiveness and efﬁciency.
Second line of defence consists in organising the process,
ensuring
the
methodological
framework,
coordinating
and
independently
supervising
the
internal
control
and
risk
management process. The functions of the second line of defence
include
speciﬁc
monitoring
mechanisms
and
processes
to
implement
the
appropriate
ﬁnancial
and
operational
risk
management system and exercise internal control. The second
line of defence is represented by the structures with functions
dedicated to ﬁnancial and operational risk management, the
Investment Committee and the Risk Committee. At this level, the
methodological framework for conducting an effective risk
management process in the NBM is developed:
the internal
framework on how to perform and control foreign exchange
operations, operational risk management by business processes,
ensuring business continuity and information security, personal
data protection,
standards,
policies and response plans to
incidents, which disrupt or threaten the operational functions of
the National Bank. An incident management system is in place at
the National Bank, where incidents are recorded, including the
measures taken and those responsible for their implementation.
Operational Risk Management Function ensured in 2021 the
organization and conduct of operational risk self-assessment
66COSO - The Committee of Sponsoring Organizations of the Treadway
Commission.
67ISO
31000
–
Risk
Management
Guide,
International
Organization
for
Standardization)


128
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
meetings on business processes. Based on the operational risk
self-assessment, the operational risk proﬁle was developed and
reported quarterly to the Risk Committee.
At the same time,
internal trainings were carried out for the representatives of the
subdivisions (heads of subdivisions, operational risk managers
and participants in the assessed processes).
In order to manage the business continuity risk, generated by
the external factor – the COVID-19 epidemic, in 2021 a series
of measures were taken to protect employees and ensure the
continuity of the NBM’s activity, a fact that contributed to the
continuous development of NBM’s activity processes, with the
fulﬁlment of all basic attributions. These measures were reported
quarterly at the meetings of the Risk Committee.
At the same time, the operational risk management function
represents the NBM at IORWG as a member.
The third line of defence provides independent and objective
assurance and is represented by internal audit.
Internal audit
provides
management
and
line
managers
with
reasonable
independent assurance on the adequacy and effectiveness of
governance, risk management and internal controls, including
how the ﬁrst and second line of defence achieve risk management
objectives.
It should be noted that the internal regulatory
framework on the operation of internal audit, which is approved at
the level of the Board of Supervisors, has been revised, aligning it
with the mandatory elements of the International Professional
Practices Framework (IPPF), developed by the Institute of Internal
Auditors (IIA). According to the external evaluation report, dated
14.03.2021 the internal audit function has been rated "General
Compliance" with the IIA Standards and Code of Ethics, which
represents the highest degree of compliance.
Internal control in the NBM also operates through a set of
regulations approved by the governing bodies and ensures that
resources are managed in an effective manner to achieve the
NBM’s mission and strategic objectives.
Framework of Compliance and Ethics
Code of Conduct for National Bank Employees
The NBM Code of Conduct for Employees operates in the National
Bank, which sets out requirements for all employees to adhere
to the highest standards of professional ethics. To this end, the
Code of Conduct addresses the ethics and professional conduct of
National Bank employees, including conﬂict-of-interest behaviour,
conﬁdentiality, security and information management.
Anti-fraud policy
The Anti-Fraud Policy of the National Bank promotes fraud risk
awareness and management.
It aims to identify areas at risk of fraud and to implement
appropriate control measures at all levels of the National Bank
that will contribute to the prevention and detection of fraud, the
monitoring
of
fraud
risk
and
the
promotion
of
consistent


Chapter 9. Institutional and organizational framework
129
organisational conduct. The anti-fraud policy also sets out the
conditions for conducting investigations into the prevention and
examination of suspected fraud.
The implementation of the anti-fraud policy, training of employees
on fraud risk and its management, reporting of suspected frauds -
all establish and develop an anti-fraud culture that deters potential
perpetrators and strengthens the commitment of National Bank
employees to combat fraud. The responsibility of all National Bank
employees has a major contribution to creating an environment
conducive to raising awareness of the risk and consequences of
fraud.
The
governing
bodies
of
the
National
Bank
promote
a
zero-tolerance policy on fraud and corruption.
Measures
to
combat
money
laundering
and
terrorist
ﬁnancing
The National Bank has established internal rules on the prevention
and combating of money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing in the
operations carried out. The purpose of these rules is not to allow
the involvement of the National Bank in operations or activities
with risk of money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing, as well as
to establish requirements for the organization, application, and
control of compliance by bank subdivisions of measures to prevent
and combat money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing.
In this context, during 2021, continued the actions to strengthen
the
central
bank’s
compliance
with
the
requirements
for
preventing
and
combating
money
laundering
and
terrorist
ﬁnancing, namely, training employees involved in enforcing the
requirements of knowledge of customers, cooperation with the
responsible subdivisions in order to determine the risks and
suspicions related to transactions and the relationship with
customers,
notifying the competent authorities in order to
determine the suspicious nature of permitting and registration of
notiﬁcations related to external loans, etc.
In 2021, the NBM
employees,
with supervisory responsibilities in the ﬁeld of
preventing and combating money laundering, beneﬁted from
international
expertise,
by
participating
in
trainings
and
workshops, including online, organized within a series of technical
assistance projects provided by international organizations. In
order to improve the internal system of compliance with the
requirements of preventing and combating money laundering, the
National Bank has implemented a real-time payment screening
service, which aims to ensure the implementation of international
restrictive measures regarding payments to its customers. The
internal
cooperation
and
reporting
system
has
also
been
strengthened in order to ensure the centralization of the NBM’s
activities to combat money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing,
including by providing timely information to the Executive Board
member, who coordinates the activity, on the identiﬁed risks as
well as relevant minimization recommendations.
For high-risk
operations, the report is done to the Executive Board.
Information security policy
The information security policy of the National Bank establishes
the general organizational context to ensure the achievement of
the objectives regarding the provision of information security and


130
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
cyber security within the NBM. It also sets out requirements for
ensuring the secure management of information within the NBM
and minimizing the impact that may arise as a result of security
incidents.
The members of the Supervisory Board and the members of the
Executive Board, as well as any employee of the National Bank, are
obliged to ensure the security of the information in their possession
and to which they have access, whatever its form. At the same
time, the responsibilities in the process of ensuring information
security are clearly established and divided, in the part related to
information management, monitoring the use and distribution of
information, reporting information security incidents, as well as
how to investigate them.
In the same context, in order to ensure information security within
the NBM, in accordance with the Information Security Policy, the
Information Security Ofﬁcer of the NBM is appointed.
Management of personal data
The National Bank processes personal data in accordance with
the Law No. 133/2011 on personal data protection. The National
Bank has effective policies, procedures and information systems
to ensure the protection of personal data. Within the National
Bank there is a designated Personal Data Protection Ofﬁcer who
is responsible for informing and advising the employees of the
National Bank, EB members in the processing of personal data,
monitoring compliance with the regulations in force, carrying out
awareness-raising and training actions for employees involved in
personal data processing operations, cooperating with the National
Centre for Personal Data Protection.
Public
access
to
normative
acts,
statistics
and
publications of the NBM
The public access to the National Bank’s normative acts, statistics
and publications ensures a high degree of transparency while
respecting the independence and conﬁdentiality of certain aspects
related to the performance of the National Bank’s tasks. Publishing
various data and analyses related to the tasks of the National Bank,
as well as promoting an open relationship with target groups are
issues of major importance in the external communication of the
bank.
The National Bank pays particular attention to the reverse link
in the communication scheme. Thus, through the regular and
frequent transmission of messages, the National Bank provides
rigorous explanations for its activity and the functioning of the
banking sector. The functioning of the interactive database on the
website of the National Bank provides public access to information,
internal data and analysis and aims to provide accurate, current,
clear, and transparent information for creating a public opinion on
the decisions of the National Bank, but also for conducting various
studies and research.


Chapter 9. Institutional and organizational framework
131
9.2
Development perspectives
The National Bank’s development prospects are linked to the
implementation of its strategic objectives.
Reforming and strengthening the non-banking ﬁnancial sector in
line with good European and international practices, by assigning
the NBM new tasks of licensing/issuing of state registration,
regulation, and supervision of the activity of entities in the
non-banking ﬁnancial sector, is one of the new development
objectives of the National Bank of Moldova, considering the entry
into force of the respective legal provisions from July 1, 2023.
The main projects of the National Bank of Moldova and the Single
Central Securities Depository planned for launch or continuation
in 2022:
• The start of the project regarding the assumption by the
NBM
of
the
regulation/supervision
attribution
of
the
non-bank ﬁnancial sector.
• The start of the project regarding the migration to new SWIFT
standards – ISO 20022.
• Continuation
of
the
Twinning
project
"Strengthening
supervision, corporate governance and risk management in
the
ﬁnancial
sector",
implemented
in
the
Republic
of
Moldova with the support of the European Union.
• Continuation of the project on the implementation of the
solution
for
streamlining
the
process
of
monitoring
shareholder transparency and remote analysis in the ﬁeld
of
preventing
and
combating
money
laundering
and
terrorist ﬁnancing, with assistance of USAID.
• Continuing to adjust the regulatory framework related to
licensing,
authorisation
and
notiﬁcation
processes,
modernizing and incorporating new modules and processes
into
the
Solution
for
Streamlining
the
Licensing,
Authorisation and Notiﬁcation Process.
• Continuation of the project on the implementation of the IT
solutions for banking operations (CBS), corporate resource
management (ERP) and cash management.
• Continuation of the project on the implementation of an
instant payment scheme.
• Continuation of the project on the modernisation of the
Automated Interbank Payment System (AIPS) - components
RTGS
(Real-time
Gross
Settlement
System)
and
DNSS
(Designated-time Net Settlement System).
• Continuation of the project on digitization of the NBM
archive.
• Continuation of the project on the expansion of connections
with trading platforms (CSD).


132
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
9.3
The strategic plan of the National
Bank of Moldova
During 2021,
the National Bank of Moldova continued the
implementation of the ﬁve strategic objectives, including 25
(twenty-ﬁve) operational/complementary/cross-cutting objectives,
from the previous strategic planning cycle.
The fundamental elements of the Strategic Plan relate to mission,
vision, values and strategic objectives.
Mission of the National Bank of Moldova
The fundamental objective of the National Bank is to ensure and
maintain price stability.
Without prejudice to its fundamental
objective, the National Bank promotes and maintains a ﬁnancial
system based on market principles and supports the economic
development of the country.
Vision of the National Bank of Moldova
The National Bank of Moldova is an initiative-taking, efﬁcient and
credible institution, guided by good governance practices.
Values of the National Bank of Moldova
Credibility – policies, reforms, communication and attitudes that
inspire conﬁdence and security in the public and markets.
Responsibility – ongoing concern about the economic and social
impact of the work done and the decisions made.
Reliability
–
human
resources,
internal
procedures
and
information technology that maintain the safety and diligence of
the functioning of the institution.
Professionalism
–
competence,
integrity,
diligence,
decision-making, continuous improvement.
Cooperation – teamwork to achieve the common goals of the
bank.
The strategic objectives derive from core business areas while the
last objective refers to support and governance:
1. To improve the monetary policy framework and optimize the
implementation of monetary policy.
2. To strengthen the function of ﬁnancial stability and macro-
prudential management.
3. To strengthen the banking sector and implement risk-based
supervision.
4. To enhance the quality of the national currency and the level
of use of cashless payments.
5. To strengthen institutional capacity and efﬁciency.
During 2021, progress was made in implementing reforms in ﬁve
key areas:
monetary policy and ﬁnancial markets, ﬁnancial
stability,
banking supervision,
payment systems and cash
operations, governance, and support. The achievements relate to
the 25 (twenty-ﬁve) operational/complementary/cross-cutting
objectives continued towards implementation, the most important
of which are included in the table below. In addition, Annex A.31
"Evolution of NBM major projects during 2021" contains the most
important achievements related to NBM projects that derive from
the strategic objectives.

Implementing

9.4
Management and organization
According to the Strategic Plan of the National Bank of Moldova,
out of the 5 strategic objectives, one is related to the human
resources ﬁeld, namely:
Strengthening institutional capacity and efﬁciency, which implies
the streamlining of NBM’s operational activity through institutional
modernization and the strengthening of internal capacities in
order to achieve the core competencies. One of the operational
objectives deriving from the strategic one is the implementation
of a new integrated human resource management system.
Respectively,
the
key
priorities
in
the
management
and
development of human resources in 2021 were focused on these
areas, the undertaken measures being further detailed.
Organizational evolutions
In 2021, there were a number of changes in the organizational
structure of the National Bank of Moldova, meant to contribute
to the achievement of the Bank’s strategic objectives and its
core competencies, but also to increase its efﬁciency by reducing
hierarchical levels.
At the same time, these changes aim to
increase the capacity of the NBM to adapt to changes in the
economic environment by redeﬁning responsibilities, restructuring
activities, and strengthening institutional capacity overall.
Thus, functions related to the basic attributions of the NBM were
developed by optimizing the subdivisions specialized in the ﬁeld of
banking supervision, ﬁnancial stability, payment systems, but also
optimizing some subdivisions related to the area of support and
governance, with a special emphasis being placed on the function
of communication and ﬁnancial education.
At the same time, several executive functions have been adapted
to the current needs of the subdivisions, without impact on the
total number of employees units.
Changes made during 2021 resulted in a slight increase in the ﬁeld
of control to 4.9 executors per manager compared to 4.7 in 2020.
Due to the changes made in 2022 in the subdivisions related to
the core business of the National Bank, the ratio of the number of
units related to the core tasks of the NBM to the number of units
related to the support and governance area in 2021 shows an
increase compared to 2020, being 55% to 45% in 2021 compared
to 53% to 47% in 2020. The optimizations conducted during 2021
had a positive impact on the efﬁcient use of the Bank’s personnel
resources.

payroll
system
currently

was
implemented
for
the
remuneration of NBM employees. Thus, the payroll system for
NBM employees provides for the establishment of a direct
relationship between the value of the position and the salary level,
as well as ensuring internal equity and external competitiveness

of pay, which entails the following beneﬁts: alignment of wages
with the reference market; internal equity (jobs of the same value
have the same pay level); external competitiveness (jobs of the

organisational climate.
Statistical information
In 2021, voluntary staff turnover was 7.13%, increasing compared
to 2020 when it registered 6.63%, the upward trend also being a
consequence of the COVID-19 pandemic,
which inﬂuenced
behavioural trends in the labour market, as well as the need to
adjust the National Bank’s human resources policy to the
changing requirements of the reference market. Analysing the
voluntary turnover by hierarchical levels, for managerial functions
in 2021 it increased compared to 2020, reaching the level of
5.83%, and for executive positions it decreased, reaching the rate
of 7.55%. It should be noted that the voluntary turnover includes
only persons who resigned on their own initiative, excluding from
the calculation persons employed on a seasonal basis and those
who left for other reasons (retirement,
termination of the
employment contract, reduction of staff, etc.), additionally it also
excluded the resignations of NBM employees whose employment
contracts were suspended in the year preceding the one in which
they actually resigned.
The average age of the Bank’s employees as of December 31,
2021, was 41 years and 7 months. Almost two-thirds are women
(61.12%), and more than one-third (38.88%) are men. We also note
that women are represented at a rate of 50% in the composition
of management positions.
Professional growth

increase the human capital of the NBM, about 63.4% of active

situation,
the
professional training of employees was conducted mainly online,
the training events being selected according to the individual
needs of professional development, starting from the training
programs provided by development partners, as well as ensuring
internal knowledge transfer.

In 2021, the NBM implemented with the support of the UK
Government and the Good Governance Fund a management
capacity building project for 70 employees in management
positions in the NBM. The project was implemented over a period
of 12 weeks, in sessions totalling 60 hours. The areas of training
were management and leadership, team management, emotional
intelligence, change management and strategic thinking. Another
strand of the project was to strengthen internal knowledge
transfer capacity.
In this context, the project trained 10 NBM
employees who participated in the facilitation and delivery of the
management training programme as well as in the preparation
and delivery of internal trainings.
The internal knowledge transfer was conducted by organizing
seminars and presentations delivered by NBM experts, speciﬁcally
dictated by NBM’s development objectives and new professional
development needs in the context of the pandemic and work from
home. At the same time, being organized online, the internal
trainings facilitated the transfer of knowledge from the specialized
subdivisions to the employees of other subdivisions on various
areas of the Bank’s activities (Chart 9.4).
At the same time, during 2021, NBM employees attended trainings
on various ﬁelds organized by the Bank’s partners and specialized
organizations, as shown in Chart 9.5.
Performance management
The Performance Management System is an instrument applied by
the National Bank of Moldova since 2016 to increase the efﬁciency
of employees’ work. Thus, following the evaluation of the results
of the employees’ activity for 2021, an increasing evolution was
found compared to the 2017 – 2020 years of the performance level
of the NBM employees.
Work environment and organizational culture
The NBM, as an employer, in the context of the COVID-19
pandemic, was in a position to face, as well as other institutions,
special challenges, namely, to take increased measures to protect
the health of employees, to ensure continuity of activity, to adapt
to the changes imposed by the new way of organizing work.
In this regard, the NBM quickly adapted to the circumstances
created by the COVID-19 pandemic and in order to protect the
health of employees, but also to ensure the continuity of NBM
activity, employees were established work from home, with the
preparation
of
the
distance
work
environment
required.
Exceptions were employees who conduct activities that require
critical presence at the NBM headquarters,
such as cash
operations, servicing the automated interbank payment system,
foreign
exchange
reserves
management,
physical
security,
administration of support services for IT users, etc.
Thus,


Chapter 9. Institutional and organizational framework
139
depending on the level of infection spread in the country and the
restrictions imposed by the Extraordinary National Commission on
Public Health in the context of the COVID-19 pandemic, NBM
employees worked at home in a proportion of 50% to 70%, with
the application of periodic cycles with bimonthly rotation. In order
to ensure a safe and protected work environment, for employees
with physical activity and presence at the headquarters were
strictly implemented measures and provided increased means of
protection and sanitation, both in the organization of workspaces
and activity and interaction of employees: limiting the meetings
with physical presence, respecting the social distance, wearing a
protective mask.
These and other measures have made it
possible to keep the rate of employee infections at a low level, the
cases of transmission of the infection between employees working
at the premises being of a singular nature.
Thus, it is worth mentioning that during the year, despite all the
pandemic challenges,
the NBM managed to fully fulﬁl its
responsibilities established by law, ensuring the continuity of
activity in both the basic and supporting ﬁelds of activity and
governance. It is worth mentioning that a crucial factor in this
regard was the high degree of automation of business processes,
as well as the electronic circuit of documents, ampliﬁed at the
beginning of the pandemic period with the implementation of
electronic
signature
and
increased
digitization
of
business
processes.
These circumstances allowed the rapid and easy
transition to the work at home regime in conditions of necessary
information security, considering the speciﬁc activity of the bank.
In addition to the measures taken for the increased protection of
employees’ health, activities and actions were organised during
the year to help strengthen the organisational culture and
maintain a sense of belonging to the institution, given the lack of
communication and interaction through physical presence at work
dictated by the pandemic. Although organised exclusively online,
these initiatives managed to create a great atmosphere of
community and team, being together, albeit at a distance. Thus,
events dedicated to the 30th anniversary of the founding of the
NBM, the annual activity report, etc. were organized. During these
events, a number of new initiatives were launched, such as the
contests "My gift to the NBM at 30th birthday", "My subdivision in
10 years", "New and old memories", "Children’s drawing contest",
etc. But a real achievement was the internal contest involving
several teams of NBM employees, where they answered curious
questions about the NBM.
At the same time, the beautiful tradition of the Charity Fair was
maintained, with employees having a highly active and socially
responsible involvement.

Recruitment
The recruitment process in the National Bank of Moldova is
characterized by a continuous effort to attract candidates to
participate in competencies to occupy vacancies of the NBM.
Considering the speciﬁcity of the bank’s vacancies, characterized
by increased requirements regarding the level of education,
complexity and expertise, but also the shortage of qualiﬁed labour
on the labour market in the Republic of Moldova, the NBM applies
diversiﬁed measures to attract candidates.
Thus,
several


140
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
specialized agencies were contracted to publish vacancy notices
and the use of social networks was intensiﬁed for this purpose.
The restrictions imposed by the pandemic have required the
recruitment process to be adapted to the online regime of running
competitions, with an increase in effort to ensure that they are
conducted.
Thus, the application of diversiﬁed measures to attract candidates,
as well as the shift to an online recruitment process, have
signiﬁcantly reduced the negative impact of the pandemic crisis.
2021 is characterised by the maintenance, and sometimes even
increase,
of
indicators
related
to
the
recruitment
process
compared to previous years. We mention that the National Bank
of
Moldova
supports
the
recruitment
and
professional
development of young specialists, as well as specialists with
experience in the ﬁelds of economics, law, information technology,
accounting, and reporting, etc.

specialists, and not only, the NBM is open to accept students
from higher education institutions in the country and abroad for
internships within the NBM. The number of interns has slightly
increased in 2021 compared to 2020. Considering the pandemic

9.6
Internal audit
The overall purpose of IAD’s (Internal Audit Department) activity
is to provide assurance, independent, and objective advisory
services, designed to add value and contribute to the improvement
of the NBM’s activities. The mission of the internal audit is to
enhance and protect the values of the NBM by providing objective
assurance based on risk, advice, and perspective approach. The
internal audit (IA) assists the NBM in achieving its objectives
through a systematic and disciplined approach to assessing and
improving the effectiveness of governance, risk management and
control processes.
The IAD is guided in its work by the mandatory elements of the
International Professional Practices Framework (IPPF), developed
by the Institute of Internal Auditors (IIA), including the Core
Principles of Professional Practice for Internal Auditing, the Code
of Ethics of the Institute of Internal Auditors (hereafter Code of
Ethics), the International Standards for the Professional Practice of
Internal Audit and the Deﬁnition of Internal Audit (hereafter IIA
Standards).
The IAD operates on the basis of the regulatory
framework68 aligned to the Supplemental Guidance "Model
Internal Audit Activity Charter" developed by the Institute of
Internal Auditors (IIA)69. Under the Qualiﬁcation Standards, the
68Regulation on the functioning of the Internal Audit Department of the National
Bank of Moldova, approved by the Decision of the Board of Supervision of the NBM
No. 32 of 12.12.2019,
69Supplemental Guidance "Model Internal Audit Activity Charter" Institute of
Internal Auditors, March 2017


Chapter 9. Institutional and organizational framework
141
quality assurance and improvement programme are designed to
provide an assessment of the compliance of internal audit work
with the IIA Standards, as well as an assessment of the application
of the Code of Ethics. Compliance with those Standards and the
Code of Ethics has been conﬁrmed by the March 17, 2021 Report
on the External Quality Assessment of Internal Audit. Based on
good practices in the ﬁeld, the independence of the IA is achieved
through
functional
subordination
to
the
NBM
Supervisory
Board/Audit Committee (AC) and administrative subordination to
the Governor of the National Bank of Moldova.
During 2021, the Audit Manual70 was revised in order to carry
out the actions of the Plan71 regarding the implementation of the
recommendations submitted by the external evaluator72, as well
as the efﬁciency of some processes related to the conduct of audit
missions.
In 2021, the audit work was conducted according to the approved
audit plan, which was developed applying the risk-based approach,
and the priorities outlined. The pandemic situation conditioned
by COVID-19 did not impose the need to modify the audit plan
for 2021. IAD in its activity did not encounter any limitations in
the presentation of the independent opinion. The Director of IAD
regularly reported the results of the audit to the governing bodies
of the NBM: the Audit Committee, the Executive Board, and the
Supervisory Board.
The annual internal audit plan for 2021 was fulﬁlled to a 100%
accomplishment73.
IAD
conducted
insurance
missions,
endorsements of the NBM’s ﬁnancial statements and budget
execution, veriﬁcation, and monitoring of high-risk areas.
Several
types
of
audits
were
conducted
-
ﬁnancial/compliance/operational (50%), integrated (29%), IT audit
(7%), monitoring (7%) and advisory (7%). Audit activities covered
45% of the processes assessed by IAD as high residual risk and
31% as medium risk. In 2021, IAD ofﬁcers participated in the role
of technical-analytical consultant in monitoring and advisory roles
in 5 major NBM projects.

Technology (hereinafter ICT), important achievements were
recorded both on the dimension related to the implementation of
major projects at the NBM level and on the technological
dimension related to the modernization and consolidation of ICT
infrastructure and services.
Within the framework of major projects at the NBM level, important
progress was noted on key projects such as those related to
