the modernisation of the automated payment system and the
implementation of the solution for streamlining the monitoring
process of shareholder transparency and remote analysis in the
area of combating money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing. A
considerable effort has also been made to successfully initiate
the project for the implementation of an instant payment system,
which will function as a catalyst for the development of payment
tools in the Republic of Moldova.
The description of all these
projects is presented in Annex A.31 "Evolution of major projects of
the NBM during 2021".
As concerns technology, projects have been developed and
implemented involving important improvements on the network
architecture and the way in which ICT resources are balanced, on
the modernization of the virtualization platform, on the expansion
of infrastructure capabilities, as well as on the improvement of
cyber protection capabilities, etc. In this context, we can mention
that the modernization of the virtualization platform is a key
element in the context of successfully implementing new projects
and maintaining existing virtualized services at an important level
of performance. For example, during 2021, processing resources
were expanded by 35% for the virtualization platforms, which
allowed us to align the processing capacities with the needs
coming from major projects in the process of implementation, in
particular the new platform for the Automated Payment System.
In addition, as part of the process of improving resilience and
optimizing the use of resources, work was conducted to rebalance
processing resources between the NBM’s data centres. On the
security side, a number of improvements were made to anti-spam
protection mechanisms, antivirus, web protection, VPN services,
etc.
in order to respond more effectively to new threats in
cyberspace.
Also in this context, it is important to mention the sustained effort
made to ensure that existing services continue to meet business
needs.
Thus, for the adjustment and development of existing
business applications, some 92 change requests for software
development were made by the development team during the
year.


143
Chapter 10
Research activity
The research activity of a central bank serves as the conceptual
and empirical basis for drawing up the policies and decisions of
the central bank. The role of research is to ensure that the central
bank has at its ﬁngertips the information needed to effectively
manage the challenges arising in its areas of activity.
To this
purpose, the Department of Economic and Applied Research
(DEAR) coordinates research activities within the National Bank
and conducts original research, analyses, develops analytical
materials based on research, on topics related to the tasks and
functions of the National Bank that serve as support in the
formulation of decisions of the National Bank in its areas of
competence.
Research activity in the National Bank is coordinated between two
working groups: economic research and applied research, each
focusing on different topical areas.
In 2021,
research activity was oriented towards providing
theoretical
and
practical
support
to
the
National
Bank’s
subdivisions in areas relevant to its tasks, functions and policies,
which included work on ﬁnancial market infrastructures, ﬁnancial
technology development, cash management, ﬁnancial inclusion
and ad hoc thematic consultations on macro-prudential and
ﬁnancial stability issues in domestic conditions inﬂuenced by
post-pandemic
consequences,
ﬁnancial
integration
and
international ﬁnancial linkages. The economic structural changes
at global, regional, and national level caused by the effects of the
health crisis and the energy crisis on the economy and the
ﬁnancial sector make it necessary to review central bank policies
and tools by creating theoretical and practical foundations
adapted to the new conditions. In this context, the role of analysis,
studies, and research in achieving the objective and basic tasks of
the National Bank is increasing.
In order to achieve the expected level of efﬁciency in terms of
academic excellence and practical usefulness, but also to bring
the National Bank’s scientiﬁc research to a level of international
recognition by ensuring the highest quality standards in research,
it is necessary to continuously invest in the development of
collaborators to enhance their expertise, knowledge of current
situations and developments in ﬁnancial markets.
Thus, during 2021, the DEAR has continuously monitored the
evolution of the national and international economic environment
in order to be able to provide an informed and research-based
opinion at any time and to be ready to develop new analytical
tools for central bank policy-setting purposes.
The DEAR actively participates in thematic working groups
organised at the initiative of other subdivisions or third-party


144
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
organisations.
In 2021, the DEAR participated in a series of
working sessions on the development of sustainable ﬁnance with
a focus on climate risk.
The DEAR supports research cooperation in the Bank and beyond,
as information exchange, discussion and debate are vital to the
research process.


145
Chapter 11
External communication
The current epidemiological and economic reality at global level
has highlighted the need to support the communication aspects of
the population, the business environment, etc., in order to increase
access to information about the decisions of the authorities and
market developments, which is useful for members of society,
especially in the new conditions.
In this regard, transparency, accessibility, predictability, and other
principles set out in the National Bank’s Communication Strategy
are important benchmarks in the interaction with target groups:
media, general public, ﬁnancial market participants, etc.
The
reverse connection with them takes place through the ofﬁcial
website, media, institutional platforms in social networks and other
information channels.
Thus, during 2021, the institution published ten press releases on
monetary policy decisions and four reports on inﬂation. The latter,
for a better understanding by citizens, were associated with the
online organization and live broadcasting of three press brieﬁngs
held by the Governor of the National Bank, as well as with the
holding of meetings of the NBM management with members of the
Economic Press Club, who later covered the subject in the media,
and with representatives of the economic civil society.
The central bank’s openness to the public and its decision-making
transparency is also reﬂected in the public consultations, which, in
the past year,
have covered 19 announcements and draft
regulations.
These
are
accessible
on
the
website
https://www.bnm.md/ – the National Bank’s main source of
information.
In total, during 2021, 1,783 information materials (press releases,
media interviews, analytical materials, normative acts, explanatory
articles, etc.) and 2,712 statistical reports were published and
updated in Romanian, English and Russian on the institution’s
website.
The analysis of views shows a further increase in the navigability
of the website and the interest of users in topics such as monetary
and exchange rate policy, banking sector supervision, the ﬁnancial
situation and its evolution, the regulatory acts developed by the
NBM, ﬁnancial education events (Table A.20).
At the same time, the data published on the National Bank’s
website are taken up and are found in journalistic materials that
constitute a comprehensive source of information for the whole
society. Thus, maintaining relations with representatives of the
media is particularly important in the communication process of
the central bank because knowledge and understanding of


146
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
macroeconomic analysis and the arguments underlying the
decisions of the monetary authority make the messages conveyed
more accessible and clearer to the public. The National Bank also
shows openness towards the media,
both proactively and
reactively, providing, within the limits of its competences, the
information requested.
In this regard, during 2021, more than 340 applications were
answered, 18 interviews were granted for magazines, information
portals, radio/TV broadcasts and eight live broadcasts of various
public events organized by the National Bank were made. The
latter concerned the launch of the International Financial Education
Week, the signing of the Cooperation Agreement between the
National Bank of Moldova and the National Bank of Romania, the
high-level international conference "Economic Recovery in Post-
Pandemic: Realities and Perspectives", the high-level international
round table "The role of a central bank in the modernization of
society – challenges in emerging economies", the event dedicated
to the 28th anniversary of the introduction of the Moldovan leu,
two events on the theme "Financial education – a personal, social
and economic beneﬁt", as well as a series of trainings on ﬁnancial-
banking topics held for the general public, etc.
These public events, organized in online and hybrid formats, were
designed as a means of digitized and highly accessible external
communication. The migration of events to the online medium,
with live broadcasting, has allowed both a wider participation of
speakers and an expansion of the audience.
The NBM has increased its efforts to train specialists writing on
economic issues. Thus, together with the Economic Press Club, it
organized a training course for young people studying economic
journalism at the Faculty of Journalism and Communication
Sciences of the State University of Moldova, including a workshop
on "How to draft a journalistic article on the ﬁnancial-banking
sector".
As a communication event, the Europe Day was also marked, when,
over several days, the public was provided with more information
on European integration, the activities conducted in this regard,
the implementation of the Association Agreement RM – EU and the
role of NBM in this process.
A special place in the communication process of the National Bank
is
also
held
by
the
subject
of
putting
into
circulation
commemorative and jubilee coins, which arouses interest for
numismatists from home and abroad, in support of the image of

In this regard, during 2021, the central bank
communicated
to
the
public
about
the
launch
of
a
commemorative coin and eight commemorative and jubilee coins
through press releases, multimedia approach (development of a

The ﬁnancial topics of general interest addressed to the public
regardless of their professional background are mainly reﬂected on
the National Bank’s social media platforms. The adaptation of the
messages related to this information channel aims to broaden the
perception in society at all levels of the central bank’s decisions
and the evolution of the ﬁnancial-banking environment. Thus, the

At the same time, in the context of the 30th anniversary of its

to the public not only the message of professional institutional
assumption, but also opened up to the citizens as a team of

Chapter 12
Financial education
Financial education has become a requirement of the present-days,
being an area of concern for general consumers worldwide. Today,
ﬁnancial concepts such as savings, credit, insurance, investment,
and many others are increasingly entering the sphere of interest
of modern people, regardless of age and profession.
In this regard, the National Bank of Moldova, like other central
banks, is taking steps to harness educational resources to give
citizens access to more ﬁnancial information.
The purpose of
these measures is to support the public in informing themselves
in order to manage their income with optimized spending, to
know the advantages of familiarizing themselves with the ﬁnancial
environment and to make informed ﬁnancial decisions.
Thus, the National Bank of Moldova conducts several ﬁnancial
education projects addressed mainly to young people, but also
to other target groups open to acquire or deepen their ﬁnancial
knowledge. At the same time, the NBM is working to cultivate
interest in this knowledge among the general public, which will
help them to ﬁnd their way more easily in the ﬁnancial area and
understand its developments.
The epidemiological context has highlighted the digitization of
processes in all areas.
New challenges have also stimulated
innovation in the ﬁnancial services sector, which requires the
consumer to adapt continuously and implicitly to ongoing changes.
Respectively, the orientation of the National Bank towards online
communication has made it possible to expand interaction with
the concerned public across the country. Thus, events such as
the "International Financial Education Week", "Financial Education
Camp" and "The Money Road" have aroused the interest of young
people in particular and have removed the limits of participation
with physical presence, facilitating access for those interested
from all the regions of the Republic of Moldova.
In the same line, the National Bank of Moldova has developed and
disseminated
for
the
public
multimedia
products
(ten
videographies, two video guides and eight podcasts), which are
available on the institution’s website and on its social media
proﬁles, including the YouTube channel, at any time convenient for
users. The topics covered include important and useful issues
about monetary policy decisions, the country’s foreign exchange
reserves,
safe payment card use,
saving,
borrowing,
and
investing, checking the security of online merchants, etc.
The dissemination of these products, along with other ﬁnancial
education activities, was also reﬂected in the public information
campaign conducted by the National Bank during 2021 under the
title "Financial education – a personal, social and economic beneﬁt".


Chapter 12. Financial education
149
At the same time, the National Bank maintained its collaboration
with civil society, further promoting the project "Make sense of
money" launched in 2019 in partnership with the Independent
Analytical Centre "Expert-grup".
The current fast-moving evolutions, especially in the ﬁnancial
environment, emphasize the need for ﬁnancial education for
citizens of all ages. Familiarity with basic concepts from an early
age facilitates later understanding of ﬁnancial concepts. The "In
the World of a Coin" event organized online by the National Bank
for pre-school children on International Children’s Day was a social
initiative in this respect.
Financial education for members of society of all ages was also
considered in the launch of the "Generation I" project in the
Republic of Moldova, launched by the NBM at an event it
organized at international level. The project was initiated by the
CFA Romania Association and has been successfully established in
the neighbouring country.
This initiative aims to encourage
individual
ﬁnancial
responsibility
by
providing
educational
resources to the general public.
In particular, the project will
support citizens in creating a long-term vision for achieving their
ﬁnancial independence.
Furthermore, the National Bank aims to unify the efforts of
authorities and civil society in the process of ﬁnancial education of
the public.
To this end, at the end of 2021, the central bank
launched a country-wide initiative to develop the concept and
draft a strategy to support the population and the development of
the national economy.
Thus, a working group was created,
represented by 15 institutions.
Financial education is a priority both for individuals and for society
as a whole. It is a key component of a complex set of necessary
state-level measures aimed at ﬁnancial inclusion, without which a
contemporary country cannot develop.


150
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
Chapter 13
Analysis
of
the
ﬁnancial
situation
at
individual
and
consolidated
level
for
2021
13.1
General considerations
According to Law No. 548/1995 on the National Bank of Moldova
(republished in the Ofﬁcial Monitor of the Republic of Moldova,
2015, No. 297-300, Article 554), the fundamental objective of the
National Bank of Moldova is to ensure and maintain price stability.
The basic tasks of the National Bank are provided in the Law on the
National Bank of Moldova. In accordance with the legal provisions,
the operations of the National Bank of Moldova are performed
for the purpose of promoting and supporting the monetary and
foreign exchange policy in the state, ensuring the stability of
the banking system, issuing the national currency, managing the
ofﬁcial reserve assets of the state, providing payment services,
and issuing electronic money, etc.
In this respect, maximising proﬁt from the activity of the National
Bank of Moldova is not an end in itself and cannot serve as an
impediment to the achievement of the Bank’s basic objective and
attributions.
The National Bank of Moldova holds 480 shares with a nominal
value of MDL 50 thousand, which represents 98.36% of the capital
of the Joint Stock Company "Single Central Securities Depository"
(hereinafter CSD or branch), an entity founded and registered on
April 4, 2018. The activity of CSD is regulated by Law on the Single
Central Securities Depository No. 234/2016.
The objective of the establishment of the CSD was to create a
central depository that would be a nodal centre for the depository
and settlement activity on the ﬁnancial market of the Republic of
Moldova and that would ensure the best conditions for its
development, with investor conﬁdence in the market being
determined by trust in the central depository.
13.2
Analysis of the ﬁnancial result at
individual level
The ﬁnancial result of the National Bank of Moldova depends
directly on the necessity to perform monetary and foreign
exchange operations in order to achieve the fundamental


Chapter 13. Analysis of the ﬁnancial situation at individual and consolidated level for 2021
151
objective and fulﬁl the basic tasks of the Bank. The evolution of
the proﬁt available for distribution, during 2019-2021, is reﬂected
in the Chart 13.1.
According to the ﬁnancial statement, as of December 31, 2021,
the National Bank recorded a proﬁt in the amount of MDL 294.27
million (2020: MDL 813.97 million), 50% of the proﬁt available for
distribution will be allocated to increase the authorized capital, up
to the level of 1/3 of the statutory capital, and the general reserve
fund, up to the level of 2/3 of the statutory capital, and 50% of
the proﬁt available for distribution will be transferred to the state
budget (Table A.21).

The main factors that determined a lower result for the previous
reporting period were:
• recording of net income from the management of
foreign exchange reserves lower by about MDL 365
million, largely as a result of the decrease in the return on
foreign exchange reserves from 0.81% in 2020 to 0.13% in
2021 generated by the gradual decrease starting from the
ﬁrst quarter of 2020 in the context of the pandemic, of the
reference rates set by the central banks of the countries in
which the National Bank of Moldova invests foreign exchange
reserves, an effect offset by the increase in foreign exchange
reserves by about 19%;
• increased costs related to the production of national
currency by about MDL 104 million, in the context of the
execution of the national currency printing program for 2021
to supplement the growing needs for national currency;
• decrease in net income from the relationship with the
Government related to monetary-currency activity by
about MDL 112 million mainly due to both the decrease in
interest income on SS by about MDL 45.69 million as a result
of changes in the portfolio structure and the gradual decrease
in interest rates in 2021 for short-term instruments, and the
increase in interest expenses on demand deposits attracted
from the Ministry of Finance by about MDL 24.00 million in
the context of the increase in cash held at the National Bank
of Moldova.
Impact of monetary policy implementation and activities
related to relations with the Government of the Republic
of Moldova on the ﬁnancial situation of the National Bank
of Moldova
The change in monetary policy instrument rates was decided
based on the identiﬁcation of the prerequisites for the urgent
conﬁguration of the conduct of monetary policy against the
background of the pandemic developments in the global and
national economy. Thus, monetary policy measures were adopted
to partially mitigate the impact of the COVID-19 pandemic on the
national economy and to prevent liquidity risk and strengthen the
stability of the banking sector in Moldova.
During 2021, the National Bank of Moldova continued to use
excess
liquidity
absorption
instruments.
Expenses
from
sterilization operations carried out through the issuance of


152
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
certiﬁcates of the National Bank of Moldova decreased, amounting
to about MDL 152 million in 2021 compared to about MDL 217
million in 2020, inﬂuenced both by the evolution of the average
interest rate on the NBC, which during the reporting period
amounted to 3.29%, compared to 3.56% in 2020, and by the
recording of an average balance of the NBC that was MDL 1.4
billion lower than during the previous year.
At the same time, the effect of these savings was inﬂuenced by the
increase in expenses related to remuneration of required reserves
by MDL 37.81 million, as a result of the increase in the average
annual rate of remuneration of required reserves in MDL up to
1.3% (2020: 0.7%).
Required reserves remuneration rates in foreign currency remained
at the same level of 0.01%.
The set of monetary policy instruments applied by the National
Bank of Moldova during 2021 and activities related to relations
with the Government generated a net proﬁt in the amount of MDL
325.97 million, compared to a net proﬁt of MDL 408.64 million in
2020 (Table A.22).
Impact of the management operations of the state’s
ofﬁcial reserve assets on the ﬁnancial situation of the
National Bank of Moldova
The ofﬁcial reserve assets of the State, held by the National Bank
of Moldova, increased as of December 31, 2021, compared to
December 31, 2020, by 3.13% or by about USD 118.34 million
(from USD 3,783.54 million to USD 3,901.88 million), being mainly
invested in USD, EUR, and GBP instruments.
At the same time, during 2021, the average annual volume of
international reserves increased by about USD 609.71 million (from
USD 3,217.03 million to USD 3,826.74 million).
Net income from the management of ofﬁcial reserve assets
amounted to MDL 246.79 million, decreasing by about MDL
364.84 million compared to the previous year, when it amounted
to MDL 611.63 million.
This result was largely driven by the
declining return on ofﬁcial reserve assets from 0.81% in 2020 to
0.13% in 2021, partially offset by the increase in the volume of
ofﬁcial reserve assets by about 19%.
The declining return on ofﬁcial reserve assets explains by lower
interest rates on ﬁnancial assets related to foreign exchange
reserves as a result of the measures implemented by the monetary
authorities of the component currencies.
During 2021, in order to combat the adverse effects of the
COVID-19 pandemic, the reference rate of the US Federal Reserve
positioned itself between the range of 0-0.25% and that of the
Bank of England positioned itself at 0.10%, increasing to 0.25%
only on December 15, 2021, in the context of inﬂationary
pressures. A similar decline was also seen in the remuneration
rates for GBP and USD funds placed in term deposits.
At the same time, the decrease in the result from the management
of ofﬁcial reserve assets was partially offset by the recording of
income from sales of foreign currency higher than the comparative


Chapter 13. Analysis of the ﬁnancial situation at individual and consolidated level for 2021
153
period (MDL +6.78 million). The recorded incomes were generated
by the positive difference between the selling rate and the cost
of the currency stock. During the period under review, the NBM
recorded foreign exchange sales in the amount of about USD
371.8 million, including sales interventions of USD 360.78 million,
which cumulatively led to the recording of realized revenues of
about MDL 171.15 million, compared to the similar period in 2020
when revenues amounted to MDL 164.37 million (in 2020, foreign
exchange sales were made in the amount of about USD 198.32
million, including sales interventions of about USD 170.47 million).
Additional information on the management of ofﬁcial reserve
assets is presented in Chapter 3, Monetary policy, Ofﬁcial reserve
assets.
The impact of ofﬁcial reserve asset management operations on
the ﬁnancial position of the National Bank of Moldova is presented
in Table A.23.

In line with the international good practices, as well as with the
provisions of the Law on the National Bank of Moldova, the main
criteria for the selection of reserve assets are safety of the base
amounts and liquidity. Thus, only after ensuring these two criteria,
the proﬁtability of the instruments in which the ofﬁcial state
reserve assets are invested, is considered.
The evolution of the ofﬁcial exchange rate of the Moldovan Leu
against the currencies in which the ofﬁcial reserve assets are held
had a signiﬁcant impact on the ﬁnancial situation of the National
Bank of Moldova (i.e., in 2021 the depreciation of the USD/MDL
exchange rate was recorded in the amount of 3.1%, compared to
the depreciation of 0.03% in 2020) (Table A.24).
At the same time, at the end of 2021, as a result of the depreciation
of the Moldovan Leu against certain component currencies of
foreign exchange stocks, net unrealized income from exchange
rate differences on the revaluation of foreign exchange stocks was
recorded in the amount of about MDL 778.39 million. At the end of
2020, the unrealized income from exchange rate differences on
revaluation of foreign exchange stocks amounted to MDL 656.31
million.

13.4
Financial result of the branch
The CSD operates, as of July 31, 2018, the Single Central Securities
Depository System, which is a securities settlement system that
ensures the provision by the Single Central Securities Depository
of securities registration, centralized administration, settlement,
as appropriate, ancillary, corporate services. The CSD operates
with government securities and the claims of the National Bank of
Moldova, with corporate ﬁnancial instruments and fully provides
the functions of settlement of stock exchange transactions. The
operation is performed by registering the securities in the CSD
system and opening accounts for the issuer and the ﬁrst owners.
The CSD settles the securities registered by the CSD. The CSD
keeps records of securities, rights and restrictions on the rights
granted by the securities.

is reﬂected in Table A.26.

13.7
Capital and reserves
In accordance with art. 19 of the Law on the National Bank of
Moldova, the Bank’s capital includes statutory capital, reserve
accounts created in accordance with the provisions of Article 66
and reserve accounts of unearned income. The statutory capital
shall include the authorised capital and the general reserve fund,
shall be dynamic and shall be formed from the proﬁt of the year
available for distribution, from the income earned in accordance
with Article 64 paragraph (3) and/or from the contributions of the
Government of the Republic of Moldova, until its size reaches 10%
of the total monetary liabilities of the National Bank of Moldova.
The General Reserve Fund may be used exclusively to cover losses
incurred in accordance with the result for the year at the end of
the ﬁnancial year, if a negative result is recorded.
According to the proﬁt allocation mechanism, if the amount of the
statutory capital of the National Bank of Moldova at the end of
the ﬁnancial year is between 4% and 10% of the total monetary
liabilities of the National Bank of Moldova, only 50% of the proﬁt
available for distribution shall be allocated to increase the statutory
capital in the manner provided for in Article 19 paragraph (3).
Considering that the level of capital on December 31, 2021, until
the distribution of proﬁt, constitutes 5.24% of the total monetary
bonds, 50% of the proﬁt available for distribution has been
allocated to increase the authorized capital, up to the level of 1/3
of the statutory capital, and the general reserve fund, up to the
level of 2/3 of the statutory capital, and 50% of the proﬁt available
for distribution will be transferred to the state budget.
The capital and reserves situation are presented in Table A.30.
Thus, as of December 31, 2021, the general reserve fund increased
by MDL 98,090 thousand, up to MDL 2,248,745 thousand while the
authorized capital increased by MDL 49,044 thousand, up to MDL
1,124,372 thousand.


156
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
Chapter 14
The Single Central Securities Depository
The S.A. Single Central Securities Depository is the branch of the
National Bank of Moldova.
The Bank holds 480 shares with a nominal value of MDL 50
thousand, which represents 98.36% of the capital of the joint-
stock company "Single Central Securities Depository" (hereinafter
CSD or branch), an entity founded and registered on April 4, 2018.
The activity of CSD is regulated by the Law on the Single Central
Securities Depository No. 234/2016.
The objective of the establishment of the CSD was to create a
central depository that would be a nodal centre for the depository
and settlement activity on the ﬁnancial market of the Republic
of Moldova and that would ensure the best conditions for its
development, with investors’ conﬁdence in the market being
determined by trust in the central depository.
In accordance with the mentioned law and the license granted by
the National Bank of Moldova, as of July 31, 2018, CSD performs
activities of initial registration of securities in the form of
book-entry, opening and administration of securities accounts,
management of the securities settlement system, clearing and
settlement of government securities and certiﬁcates issued by the
National Bank of Moldova. As of May 1, 2019, CSD also performs
settlement of corporate securities of joint-stock companies.
The rigors and principles of the internal control system of the
branch are prescribed in the Law 234/2016 and it is organized
and managed based on internal control policies, approved by the
Supervisory Board of CSD, periodically reviewed.
The main instrument of internal control are the procedures, which
establish and describe the responsibilities of persons involved in
the implementation of the process or activity that is the subject of
the procedure. In order to minimize operational risks, CSD applies
the "four eyes" mechanism.
In order to strengthen the internal control framework, maps of
the main business processes are developed and implemented,
setting out the steps and measures to be taken by CSD staff in the
execution of operations.
The branch’s internal control system aligns the governance of the
internal control system and operational risk management with
international COSO standards, ISO 31000, best practices in the
ﬁeld. In the area of compliance, the branch has an internal unit,
independent in the exercise of its functions and reporting to the


Chapter 14. The Single Central Securities Depository
157
Supervisory Board of the branch. CSD Compliance Policies are
applied, and regular compliance assessments are carried out.
The branch has implemented internal and external audit functions.
IT internal audit is outsourced for the period 2020-2023 to Grant
Thornton. Internal audit on other areas is performed by an internal,
independent unit reporting directly to the Supervisory Board of
the branch.
IT maintenance and support services in the area
of operation of the Single Central Depository System (Depo/x)
and information security are outsourced to the National Bank of
Moldova.
For 2020-2024, the AGA has appointed BAKER TILLY MOLDOVA as
the external auditor of the ﬁnancial statements of the CSD. For
2020-2021, audit reports with opinions without qualiﬁcation have
been issued.
The Central Depository is supervised and regulated by the National
Bank of Moldova.
In 2021, a thematic audit of the CSD was
conducted by the NBM, which assessed the implementation of
the Business Plan for 2020 and some NBM decisions.
The NCFM regulates and supervises the activity of the branch
in the ﬁeld of preventing and combating money laundering and
terrorist ﬁnancing.
The General Meeting of Shareholders is the supreme governing
body of the CSD. In 2021, 2 general meetings were held: ordinary
and extraordinary.
The CSD has collegial governing bodies: the Supervisory Board
and the Executive Board. The Supervisory Board of the Central
Depository consists of 7 members, appointed by the general
meeting of shareholders, of which 4 members are appointed by
the National Bank, 1 member by the Ministry of Finance, 1 member
by the National Commission for Financial Markets and 1 member
by the Stock Exchange.

In order to perform its duties, 15 meetings of the Executive Board
were convened during 2021, at which 56 resolutions were
examined and approved, and 9 meetings of the Supervisory Board
were convened, at which 56 resolutions were approved.
The
decisions of the governing bodies were taken on the basis of the
powers assigned by law and the Articles of Association.
In 2021,
the CSD managed to achieve some performance
indicators regarding the activities carried out. In this regard the
following main achievements of the CSD can be noted:
• development and approval of 12 new internal regulations,
including reconciliation procedures, procedures applied in
the event of insolvency, continuity plans, Regulation on
procedures in the ﬁeld of PFMLTF, etc.;
• expanding international collaboration activities, including
obtaining by CSD the full ECSDA membership and initiating
collaboration with the Romanian Central Depository to create
connections;
• creating and implementing the corporate bond registration
mechanism and registering of 3 corporate issues in the CSD
System;


158
Annual Report (NBM, 2021)
• integration with the mPay platform, with the possibility of
online payment of the services offered by CSD to individuals;
• strengthening of the internal compliance and internal audit
framework, including the employment of the compliance
ofﬁcer, internal auditor, and the creation of committees under
the CSD Supervisory Board;
• strengthening of the internal information technologies and
continuity framework (IT rights and resources tracking,
implementation of continuity plans);
• creation of the (internal) encumbrance registers and the
relevant framework for keeping track of the insurance
measures applied to securities registered in the CSD system;
• increase the volume of settlement operations with SS and
CBN, increase the number of services and operations with CS,
conduct operations in the CSD System in a secure manner.
State securities and NBC
In 2021, the CSD made ISIN registrations and allocations for a total
of 174 government securities and NBM certiﬁcates, redemptions
of 194 securities. Settlement of all transactions performed by
NBM, as well as secondary market transactions conducted by CSD
participants and their clients, was ensured.
During the reporting period, 6,253 transfer orders were settled
in the SS System, which were based on both the delivery versus
payment (DVP) and delivery free of payment (FoP) principles. In
terms of the value of the settled transactions, the total amount
of these transactions amounted to approximately MDL 313,274
million, exceeding by 11.10% the amount of transactions settled
in 2020.
Corporate securities
During 2021,
7 corporate instruments were registered.
3
instruments were registered with maintaining the ISIN code
previously allocated, 4 instruments were registered with ISIN code
allocation according to ISO 6166, of which, for the ﬁrst time for
the capital market of the Republic of Moldova and CSD, 3
corporate instruments of the municipal bond type were registered.
Additional securities issuance operations were performed for 8
issuers
and
securities
placement
operations
for
3
issuers,
including the registration and placement of municipal bonds.
The additional issuance and placement of securities did not involve
the settlement of funds through the CSD System.
In 2021, 178 transactions concluded at the MSE, in the amount of
MDL 39.37 million (the transactions were settled under the DvP2
mechanism on T+2) and 770 OTC transactions were settled, of
which 121 were performed through the participants of the CSD,
and another 649 settlements were conducted directly by the CSD.
Development perspectives
The continuous development of the CSD System and the provision
of new facilities for securities transactions is a development priority


Chapter 14. The Single Central Securities Depository
159
of the Central Depository. The Central Depository envisages a
number of projects that innovate, adapt, and meet the needs of
ﬁnancial market development.
The Central Depository is focusing its efforts on strengthening its
core business areas, as well as its governance and support areas,
and is prioritizing them overall:
1. Development and strengthening of key functions;
2. Optimizing the securities settlement framework;
3. International cooperation and collaboration with the business
community;
4. Development of new business projects and directions;
5. Development and expansion of operational activity.
The analysis of CSD’s ﬁnancial activity for the ﬁnancial year ended
on December 31, 2021, is presented in Chapter 13.

Chapter 15
Other aspects of social responsibility
Environmental protection
The NBM’s actions in the ﬁeld of environmental protection are
reﬂected in its actions to reduce the amount of paper consumed
by
means
of
digitizing
processes
and
signing
documents
electronically,
reducing
waste
and
recycling
it,
promoting
sustainable public procurement, purchasing only electrical and
electronic equipment from manufacturers that are registered in
the "List of Manufacturers" of products subject to extended
producer responsibility regulations, held by the Environment
Agency, modernising electrical installations, rational use of energy
resources,
building
repairs
that
will
ensure
higher
energy
efﬁciency, using cleaning products that are ECOLABEL certiﬁed or

Cultural Activities
The National Bank issues commemorative coins in the series
"Personalities",
"Holidays,
culture,
traditions
of
Moldova",
"Monuments of Moldova" and "Historical events". These coins
reveal outstanding ﬁgures of national culture, places of historical
importance or local traditions.
In 2021, a special set of commemorative coins was put into
circulation, dedicated to the 30th anniversary of the proclamation
of the independence of the Republic of Moldova.
They will
complete the "Historical events" series.
The coins have the
signiﬁcance of a 30-year historical journey: past, present, and
future.
Each coin reﬂects famous titles of verses that have
become ballads: "O libertate, sfanta libertate" (author of the poem
"Libertate" – Ion Hadarca; composer - Anatol Dumitras; interpreter
– Angela Bucico), "Inima mea e Moldova" (lyrics - Simion Ghimpu;
composer - Ion Aldea-Teodorovici; interpreter – Ion Suruceanu) and
" Cat traim pe acest pamant" (lyrics – Nicolae Dabija; composer –
Tudor Chiriac; interpreter – Alex Calancea Band).
Responsibility to the community
The NBM encourages its employees to participate in charitable or
voluntary actions. Employment at the National Bank of Moldova is
on a competitive basis. To this end, the institution recruits talented
and enthusiastic employees through career fairs, advertisements
published on the NBM website, specialized portals, social and
social networks or in the print media, offering them opportunities
to increase their skills at work.

Chapter A. Statistical tables
177
Table A.13: Investment limits and constraints applied by the NBM in the investment risk management process
Risks
Diminution methods
* Investing in safe, high creditworthiness counterparties, established by the international rating agencies Standard & Poor’s, Moody’s and
Fitch Ratings (the average rating of the three agencies’ quotations applies, estimated on the basis of at least two quotations).
* Establishing a minimum permissible rating of counterparties authorized to conduct transactions.
Credit risk
* Establishing limits on investment instruments, counterparties/issuers, deviation from the benchmark and daily monitoring of their compliance.
* Quantiﬁcation of credit risk, using the default probabilities estimated by the rating agency Standard & Poor’s, for each type of rating and
depending on the residual maturity of the ﬁnancial instruments.
* Monthly calculation of expected credit losses in accordance with IFRS 9.
* The daily monitoring of the market risk is performed through the evaluation at the market price of the unrealized income/losses from the
ﬂuctuation of the exchange rates and from the modiﬁcation of the securities prices.
* The market risk related to the investment portfolio is assessed based on the risk value methodology (VaR and CVaR), which estimates the
potential ﬁnancial losses, based on set parameters and assumptions regarding adverse developments in market conditions.
* The monitoring of the interest rate risk and the determination of the sensitivity of the price of the securities from the investment portfolios
to the change of the interest rates is performed by applying the indicator PV01.
Market risk
* Establishment by Executive Board of the NBM the normative foreign exchange composition of the investment portfolio aimed at hedging
foreign exchange risk and optimizing the portfolio through an adequate correlation of the foreign exchange composition of assets and liabilities,
as well as an acceptable investment horizon to ensure the continuity of serving current external obligations and to implement the state’s
monetary and foreign exchange policies. The normative foreign exchange structure is determined based on an ample analysis of
the foreign exchange composition of the trade of goods and services, the external debt, the currency in which the interventions are performed,
etc. The foreign exchange composition of reserves may deviate within +/- 10 percentage points from the normative foreign exchange structure.
* Establishing by the NBM Investment Board the foreign exchange structure of each sub-portfolio and permissible deviations.
* Compliance with the normative foreign exchange composition and the foreign exchange structure of each sub-portfolio within the permissible
limits of deviation.
* Ensuring liquidity through the substantial share of demand deposits and investments in securities with high liquidity and low level of risk.
* Establishing the duration limits on sub-portfolios and the maximum maturity of investments depending on the instrument.
* In the case of indexed investment sub-portfolios, the daily monitoring of liquidity risk is also performed by applying benchmarks depending
Liquidity risk
on the type of instruments and currency. Benchmarks are market indices, recognized and used worldwide in the comparative assessment of the
performance and risks of investment portfolios.
* The investment policy of the NBM allows the active management of reserves, with limited deviations of instruments and duration from the
benchmark.
Source: NBM
